# Finale arc: m09 and m10

Stage 0, shared document for both finale missions. Author: stage-0 author (Opus), 2026-10-20. Status: round 1–4 findings folded in (§10–12, §14); stage 1 handover in §13; stage 0 complete. Stages 1–2 changes from both missions folded in by the arc-keeper, 2026-10-21 (§15); stage 3 started from that version. Stage 3 (moral choices) changes from both missions folded in by the arc-keeper, 2026-10-21 (§16), with user decisions F12 (m09's Alpha-tapes clip is voiced) and F13 (surrender is a deal with no public trial; arrest is a public trial with no cells). Where this document and the stage 3 documents (`m09.../stages/stage_3/moral_choices.md`, "m09 MC"; `m10.../stages/stage_3/moral_choices.md`, "m10 MC") disagree, the stage 3 documents win. The stages 0–3 milestone fixes (both milestone reviews, user decisions F14–F16 and the orchestrator's simplification rulings) were applied by the arc-keeper on 2026-10-21 (§17); where they change a stage 3 decision, this document now leads until the mission documents are folded. Stages 4–5 (objectives and rooms) closed for both missions on 2026-10-21, and their changes at this document's level were folded in by the arc-keeper the same day (§18), with user decisions F10 and F17–F20 and the orchestrator's rulings; the stage 4 and 5 documents (`m09.../stages/stage_4/objectives_tasks.md` and `stage_5/room_layout.md`, "m09 S4" / "m09 RL"; the same for m10, "m10 OT" / "m10 RL") are the authority for rooms, locks, wiring and engine verdicts. The stages 4–5 milestone fixes were applied by the arc-keeper on 2026-10-21 (§19), with the stage 6 and stage 7 handover lists at the end of §13. Stage 6 (lore and every readable) closed for both missions on 2026-10-21, and its changes at this document's level were folded in by the arc-keeper the same day (§20); the two stage 6 documents (`m09.../stages/stage_6/lore_fragments.md`, "m09 S6"; the same for m10, "m10 S6") are the authority for every readable string, code value and item id, and where a string quoted here differs from them, they win. Stage 7 (ink) was folded in on 2026-10-21 (§21). Stage 8 (review) was folded in by the arc-keeper on 2026-10-21 under the orchestrator's `STAGE_8_RULINGS.md` (§22); bible items the finale extends or contradicts are collected in `BIBLE_NOTES_FOR_LATER.md` in this folder, and the bible itself is not edited.

Inputs: `docs/agents/FINALE_BRIEF.md`, the two carry-over inventories in this folder (A: m01–m04, B: m05–m08), `season_1_arc.md` Missions 9–10 (old intent only), `the_architect.md` identity section, m08's closing debrief ink. Per-mission stage 0 files point back here rather than repeating it:

- `../m09_digital_archaeology/stages/stage_0_initialization.md`
- `../m10_full_disclosure/stages/stage_0_initialization.md` (folder renamed from the working id `m10_final_cipher`)

The VM plan is written separately (`finale/FINALE_VM_PLAN.md`). This document gives each flag's story meaning and where it is submitted, nothing more.

## 1. The shape of the finale

### 1.1 Where the player starts

m08 ends on day nine after Portland. Nightshade is in police hands, kept as a triple agent, or (rarely) handed over silent after a knock-out. The Global Threat Database, "every vulnerability SAFETYNET has ever catalogued", went to Tomb Gamma. Netherton: "Take your seventy-two hours. Then we go to Montana and we get it back." The coordinate (an old Cold War bunker in Montana) is known on every route except the knock-out before the fate. Nobody has said Tesseract's name aloud since m06, and only a player who opened Satoshi's safe heard HaX say it then, as his 87% guess. A player who asked Netherton about the line in m08 heard him promise not to say a name "until we can prove it"; most players never asked. HaX's last text, on every branch except the knock-out before the fate: "Same classroom, 0x00. Don't ask me whose yet." (On that branch, and on a cold start, m09's briefing gives HaX "Sorry about the leave. This is why." instead of "Keep not asking."; `m09_briefing.ink` beat 4; stage 8 SC 10.)

So the finale owes the player four things: the name, said aloud and proved; the bunker; the database; and a reckoning with eight missions of their own choices.

### 1.2 What the Architect is doing: the Publication

He did not steal the database to sell it. The old outline's "global vulnerability auction" makes him a dealer, and nothing in m01–m08 sounds like a dealer. His last directive ended: "Entropy is inevitable. Systems decay. I merely publish the schedule. -- A." (m07 decode).

**The Publication:** Tomb Gamma gives the whole stolen database away, free, every entry with working detail, in one of two ways. By default it goes out **city by city, starting at 06:00 UTC** on day 13. A key at the core's release console sends **everything, everywhere, in the same minute**, and the key needs a hand on it (m09 §14 change 28). His case is the one he left SAFETYNET over: services like SAFETYNET hoard weaknesses instead of getting them fixed, the arms race speeds up the collapse it claims to prevent, and only a shock everyone feels at once forces the fix. "Let it hurt afterwards, not during" turned inside out: let it hurt now, everywhere, so that afterwards no one can hoard.

**Full disclosure is not the Publication** (milestone D-02). m10 is called *Full Disclosure*, and the irony stays, but a student must not leave thinking the security community's full-disclosure practice is the villain's. Full disclosure is one researcher publishing one flaw, usually after a vendor has stopped answering; the Publication is every working exploit released at once, with no warning to anyone, priced in deaths. One line draws it, once per mission: m09 at flag 4's read of the Publication (HaX, setting the notice's coordinated disclosure apart too; wording in m09 SA Beat 3.5), m10 at the briefing (Netherton): "Full disclosure is one researcher, one flaw, after the vendor stops answering. This is every weapon we hold, handed out at once." The player's own option is named **coordinated disclosure**, in the button text at the database menu and in the line before it (§4.1 step 4), and m09's notice is introduced the same way.

**Why he needs Agent 0x00.** The Publication only proves his point if a SAFETYNET agent presses the key: the service that hoarded the map gives it away with its own hand. His file on the player (the Reading, m09) ends with a prediction that 0x00 will be the one. That is why he invites the raid instead of moving the hour, why one door is keyed to the player's name, why he waits at the console and does not fight for it, why he is in no hurry when 0x00 walks into the core ("Take your time. It goes at six whatever we say. Or now, if someone presses this. I'd rather it was you."; he does not pause anything, m10 change 37), and why Join is on offer at all. Stopping him is no loss to him either: stopped, he gets a courtroom and a platform, and he says so in his case, which makes his calm at the console strategy rather than passivity: "Either you press it, or I get a courtroom. I've costed both." (milestone M-10). Surrender and lethal force are the two endings that deny him both, which is what makes them the player's win over him. If nobody presses, the fallback releases the database on its own from 06:00 UTC, city by city. That keeps the clock real for the raid and makes his lesson muddier, and he hates that. His own sleeper on the raid team doesn't count: "When they find out what you are, it proves nothing. It has to be one of theirs." (§4.4; m10 change 27.)

**The cells' share.** The cells get the database two hours early, as their reward for their parts in the theft: the head start is a push from the core set for 04:00 UTC on the raid's countdown. In play it is driven by story events, not wall-clock time (§4.2): it was always set for 04:00, flag 2 is where the player finds it, and every live share goes at one moment, the core's outer lock's unlock event, which the clock calls 04:00 (m10 change 19; m10 MC P29). The server-hall console can stop one share only, or let Zero Day's run and trace it ("I can kill one feed, or follow one. Not both. Pick."); the other share is stopped, if at all, in its own scene (Ghost on the handset, Zero Day in the gallery). That is why Zero Day sends a representative and Ghost is selling the head start on. The bunker's guards are the cells' people, and nobody told them why the door opened for 0x00: "They think you're here to steal their head start. You are."

He has costed it, as he costs everything. His projection, signed in his hand, prices both modes: "Release by key, all cities: 1,100 to 2,600. Fallback by city: fewer, later." That is 1,100–2,600 dead in the first month if the key is pressed, as hospitals, grids and dispatch centres are hit by people who never had the skill before; the fallback kills fewer, and later. "An acceptable price for the last arms race." m09's flag 4 gives the player his original sheet; m10's workshop holds a later printing that carries Asante's correction (the model credits the hospitals' recovery twice, so it understates the dead) and his second signature under the unchanged figure (m09 changes 5, 28; m10 changes 12, 29, 42). Stage 6 fixed the sheet's text (m09 S6 `mirror_job_printout`; m10 S6 §1.4): the heading "PROJECTED DEATHS ATTRIBUTABLE TO THE PUBLICATION, FIRST THIRTY DAYS", then "Release by key, all cities: 1,100 to 2,600." / "Fallback by city: fewer, later." / "Principal causes: hospital systems (dispatch, infusion, records); grid control; water treatment." / "Assumes the cells move first." / "An acceptable price for the last arms race. -- A." m09's printout adds the release configuration ("Manual, at console. Single key. Requires an operator present." / "Fallback: 06:00 UTC, staged by city."). m10's bench copy is "Printing 3. Refit model." with the heading and five lines word for word, Asante's margin correction in a second hand ("Hospital recovery is counted twice. This understates. A.A.") and his "Seen. -- A." under it. Her figure, **2,100 to 3,600** in the first thirty days, is on her coded margin strip and in her model, not on the sheet (m10 S6 §1.4; the Council's own curve reads 2,000 to 3,400 at week 4). That is the escalated canon's true believer with a spreadsheet, and it is also a real argument about vulnerability disclosure, which is the educational spine of the finale (responsible disclosure vs hoarding vs full release). Both missions teach disclosure at its true speed (m09 MC §3.5, m10 MC §6.5; m10 MC P30): a notice is acknowledged within hours, a fix takes about ninety days, and fitting it takes a year or more, with some sites never patched; some flaws can't be patched this year or at all, so those go to operators with workarounds, not into a public list. That is why the service's case for holding is real too, and why disclosure has a public cost on the night it is chosen.

Why this works for the season:
- It explains the four-site night as theatre for the theft (m08) without retconning m07's "he measured you" reading: both were true.
- It makes the m02 Ghost ("Not the money. The lesson") and the m04 disclosure call into the same argument the player now has with the man who started it.
- It gives Scorched Earth a real logic: destroy both copies and nobody holds the map, SAFETYNET included. (Nobody fixes anything either; §4.5.)
- It gives Join ENTROPY a real logic: a player who agrees with him can press the button.

### 1.3 Purpose of each mission

**m09 Digital Archaeology: the name and the way in.** SAFETYNET has read Tesseract's order, "Burn Helena before the Publication. Schedule attached. -- A.", and let it go on so as not to tip him (m09 changes 2, 9). The day the leave is cut short, the player goes to Helena, Montana, to a commercial records archive that holds the bunker's deeds and construction file (the bunker is the **Sun River Regional Control Site**, a 1962 civil defence control site in Lewis and Clark County, leased from the state on June 2, 2016 to **Big Sky Holdings LLC** and refitted last year; m09 S6 `lease_file`, m10 S6 §4.4). It is also where the Insider Threat Initiative keeps its records, and where the Architect stored the papers of his two closed workshops, Tomb Alpha and Tomb Beta. The player digs through decommissioned records (paper stacks, a tape room, the archive's servers) to (1) confirm the Architect is Dr Adrian Tesseract, from his own hand; (2) find the Initiative's ledger of placements, which names a sleeper inside SAFETYNET; (3) get the plans of Tomb Gamma's defences; (4) learn the Publication's hour. On the way the player finds a flaw SAFETYNET catalogued nine years ago and never reported: one of the holes Critical Mass later came through at Albion. It is in the vendor's controller (Carrow Controls' RM-4 remote-management module, catalogue ref VH-2017-0312, held since 2017 under "Disposition: RETAIN. Operational value. Review 2019."; his memo is dated 14 March 2017; m09 S6 `albion_folder`), which Albion has since fixed on its own site but which also runs in water works and substations, about four hundred sites still open, and SAFETYNET's watch on Critical Mass runs there (m09 MC §3.5). With it are Tesseract's memo from his last years as chief strategist, arguing it should go to the vendor, and the vendor notice he drafted and never sent. His case is partly true, and the player learns that before he makes it. Every player decides whether to send the notice, nine years late: through the drop terminal, from HaX's hub on the player's word, any time after flag 3 (m09 S4 P8), or, if still unsent, when Netherton hands it to them at the debrief. Sending starts a fix that takes about ninety days and a year to reach the sites, and SAFETYNET pulls the Critical Mass watch at once; on m04 `full` or `crew_lost` it also lets Albion's inquiry ask when SAFETYNET knew (exported as `albion_notified`; m09 changes 6–7 in m09 MC §1). The order's destruction schedule, in Sorensen's safe, leaves Tomb Beta's server off (it stands in the salvage cage behind the tape room, m09 S4 P1): the server holding his credential, his memo, the hour and the defences is left running on purpose, because he wants to be known and wants them to come (m09 change 2). It ends with Tesseract's first call to HaX, and the raid order.

The night has its own clock: Tesseract has ordered Helena burned before the Publication. The purge is already running when the player arrives: Sorensen is shredding behind the mesh of the restricted cage and Jesse Tran is wiping tapes from the server closet. It moves on story events, not minutes, so being slow at the VM costs nothing. It is visible, and twice the player must choose what survives: at the cage (one bin; the other is gone when the cage opens) and at the Recruiter's call, whose price is the whole deck (take her name and both tapes go; refuse, and HaX saves one tape, her file or the Alpha tapes). Once the cage is open, stopping Sorensen by force before a clock HaX names saves her last box, the legacy placement files; talking her down gets her account of what she destroyed instead; stopping Tran saves the rest of the tape side (§6.1). Nothing required is ever lost. The raid team is introduced at the m09 briefing, and the ledger tells every player that one of them is a plant.

Feel: investigation under a purge, dread, a building full of other people's secrets including the player's own. Light combat. The player's file is in the archive. Ten rooms on one map, with no corridor (m09 RL §1–2). About 85 minutes with the field guides (about 57 in the game and 28 at the VM, the VM figure an estimate until timed; about 76 on the critical path), 95–100 without; stage 8's playtests set the published figure (m09 change 14; m09 S4 §11).

**m10 *Full Disclosure* (id `m10_full_disclosure`; the bunker is Tomb Gamma): the raid and the reckoning.** Task Force Null goes into the bunker the night before the Publication. The player's history decides which doors are already open, who is on the inside, who is waiting, and who turns. At the core they meet Tesseract in person, stop or allow the Publication, decide what happens to both copies of the database, and decide how it ends for him.

Feel: the season's biggest set piece, a siege in a Cold War bunker, with faces from earlier missions. Five full scenes run on every combination of choices, with different people by branch (§5.3): the door (Nightshade), the gallery (Victoria, or Pieter Vos), the power room (Voltage, or the technician Wendell Briggs), the workshop (Gary, or the technician Imran Siddiqui) and the network (Ghost). A player who won a branch meets that person as an ally or a prisoner; one who lost meets them as an obstacle. The rest are presences, props and lines. A short whodunit runs through it: which of the raid team is the sleeper, WREN. Not hard: every route works with all defaults, and the carry-overs mostly add help. About 105 minutes end to end, ceiling 110 (m07 ships at 90), with the close budgeted at about 8 minutes; the media room is cut now, not at stage 8 (milestone ruling, D-05), so m10 has eight playable rooms, in one corridor from the surface to the core (m10 RL §1). Of the 105 minutes about 32 are the VM, an estimate from m07 and m08 until a timed run before stage 7 (m10 OT §8).

### 1.4 Why not the old Tomb Alpha / Tomb Beta premise

The old outline (`season_1_arc.md` ~L815) sends the player to two abandoned bases before Tomb Gamma. Reworked, not dropped:

- m08's debrief gives the player a clock: "Montana in seventy-two hours, Agent. Tomb Gamma." at the door (or "Take your seventy-two hours. Then we go to Montana and we get it back." for a player who asked what next; on the no-coordinate branch, "Seventy-two hours, Agent. Then we find Tomb Gamma."; `m08_closing_debrief.ink` `the_next`, `close`; stage 8 SC 10). A detour to two other states' ruins first (Tomb Alpha was Detroit, Tomb Beta New Mexico) breaks that clock. Helena is on the way: same state, same days.
- Nightshade's go-bag held a one-way ticket to Helena for the morning after Portland (m08). Helena is already in canon as the waypoint to the bunker. On `m08_nightshade_tidied` the holdall was gone before SAFETYNET reached his locker, so m09 says it through the airline instead: "He cleared his locker before we reached it. The airline kept his booking: one way, to Helena." (stage 8 SC 4; row 116).
- Two abandoned sites cost two maps for one mission. One archive with the Alpha and Beta material in it (their files, the Alpha tapes in the tape room, and Tomb Beta's last server among the salvaged kit in a cage behind it) keeps the archaeology and the history at one map's cost.
- "Everything the cells field has been through Gamma first. Including the people" (m07 dossier). The Initiative's records sitting next to the Architect's own is the "including the people" made physical, and it connects m05's forty-seven and m08's "there may be others" in one room.
- The no-coordinate route (Nightshade knocked out before his fate) gets its fallback for free: the archive holds the deed, so m09 finds the bunker for that player.

### 1.5 Timeline

The Citadel keeps UTC; days count from the Portland order (m08 CONTRACT).

**The in-world year is 2026** (orchestrator ruling at m09 stage 6, checked against m01–m08 and the bible: m01–m02 are 2024, m06's data runs to May 2025; m09 S6 §3.1). No string in either mission prints the present month; the season is left to the cold and the dark. Past documents carry their own dates: Nightshade's intake and HaX's STRAT-204 course notebook, 2011 ("fifteen years ago"; the notebook is dated 17 October 2011); WREN placed through Harwick & Lowe, 2015 ("eleven years"; file HL2015-0477); the Albion flaw held, 2017 ("nine years"); Tesseract left, 2019 ("seven years"); the bunker leased June 2, 2016 (US date order on American paperwork); St. Catherine's ransom, November 2024.

| Day | Event |
|---|---|
| 9 | m08. |
| 10 | Leave starts. |
| 11 | m09: leave cut short when SAFETYNET reads Tesseract's order to burn Helena before the Publication and lets it go on (the courier label names the archive; how the courier was caught, by branch: the Calder Wharf courier account, else the HashChain tags, else the Helena ticket in Nightshade's go-bag). Briefing on the morning of day 11 (UTC). The night operation at the Helena archive is the local night of day 11, which is early day 12 on the Citadel's clock; in-game lines say "on day ten" for WREN's ledger update and "at the briefing" for the morning's events (m09 change 26). Ends with the Publication hour: 06:00 UTC on day 13, about twenty hours after the hangar debrief. |
| 12, night | m10: the raid on Tomb Gamma. The evening of day 12 in Montana; on the Citadel's clock, the early hours of day 13 (about 01:45–06:00 UTC), which is what every in-game screen shows. |
| After | Epilogue in each ending; then the teaser slot; then credits. |

Dispatch in eleven counties has been dropping calls since day nine for every player who finished m07 without covering Austin. That keeps running through both missions and is part of the stakes (§2, m07). A player who never played m07 gets m07's never-played canon (§3): Austin was covered and the dispatch line doesn't run.

### 1.6 Tone and difficulty

TV spy thriller, season finale. Violence at that level and brief: knock-outs, and two lethal-force choices (user decision F11), each described in one narrated line: a justified one when he makes a move that threatens lives, and a cold one offered separately, clearly labelled and confirmed (§4.1 step 5). No eldritch content anywhere, except the optional teaser (§7). Fun and educational, not hard: carry-overs add routes and help far more often than they add obstacles, and every obstacle a carry-over adds has an in-mission way round.

## 2. Thread allocation

One row per decision or thread from both inventories. **Pays off**: m09, m10, both, epilogue (the m10 debrief and closing beats), or dropped (with the reason). **Weight**: H / M / L, how much the player feels it. **Import** gives the name in the reading mission; a ✱ means the source mission must add it to `exportGlobals` first (§3.3), ✱✱ means that also needs an ink change, which goes to the user.

Three devices carry most of the payoffs, so the player meets their history as things happening, not a debrief roll-call:

- **The Reading** (m09). The Architect's own file on Agent 0x00 sits in the archive: his reading of the player, the thing he promised in m07 ("You'll have the figures too"). It is short: four entries at most, chosen by priority (m01 `final_choice`; the innocents line; m04 `disclosure_choice`; m07 `debrief_stance`), and a last page that predicts 0x00 will press the Publication's key (§1.2), with one pencilled margin line, the same for every player and outside the four entries: "They will save Lawson's file. They will want to know why he did it." (W-26; m09 MC §4.6). It is right on `cage_bin = derek_file` and wrong otherwise, which m10 pays ("Then I was wrong about two things."). Sorensen, bluffed or cornered, quotes one entry at the player aloud before they ever read it. Fixed strings, narrator voice or unvoiced.
- **Who is in the bunker** (m10). Five full scenes of two or three minutes run for every player, each on the main path, and each slot holds a different person by branch (§5.3): the door, the gallery, the power room, the workshop and the network. **Winning a branch puts a person in the room too**, each in their own room (§5.3): an arrested Nightshade is brought to the forward post in cuffs (the only slot there); a protected Gary is placed in the workshop; a captured Voltage is brought up after the breach and met at the power room; an arrested Victoria rings on a secure line in the server hall (optional, with a warned cost), and her information is used automatically, face to face with Vos in the gallery (D-04). The rest are presences (Mercer in the server hall), props (Reeves on the rota sheet, Hollis on the duty board) or lines. Every helper is named aloud as the player's doing at the moment it helps.
- **Tesseract's case, and the player's answers** (m10 confrontation). After the Publication and the offer, his evidence opens "I told you once I prefer to watch. I've watched." and uses three record lines at most, by priority (m04 disclosure, m01 final choice, m07 stance). The talk-down is three exchanges; under each of his claims the player may see up to two answers from their own record, each record slot drawing on its own pool, most specific first (m10 MC §6.3), so the player brings up their own history. Never a requirement.

### 2.5 m05 Insider Trading

| # | Thread / decision | Pays off | How the player feels it | Wt | Import |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 60 | The Recruiter (free, "the next one might cost me more") | m09 | m09's ENTROPY cell is hers: the Helena archive is the Insider Threat Initiative's records office. She rings the player's handset in m09 (phone only, as in m05) once **flag 2 is in and the cage has been opened**, after a short delay, outside the cage and not during another conversation, so the player already knows from the ledger that WREN is on the team and Sorensen is never in the scene. Every player gets the call, and **every player gets her offer**. Her reason, as accounting: the Architect has taken WREN's *tasking* for Montana (WREN still reports to the Initiative and draws its money, but her orders now come from him), and his order is burning her records office, so she sells what she can no longer use (m09 change 8). The offer: WREN's name, now, always right (trusting her is never a trap), if the player lets her file be wiped. Her file is a tape on a deck she wipes herself, from the deck's controller in Sorensen's office, as she hangs up; it holds her handler list, which HaX names before the player answers: by m05's outcome, the handlers for the twenty-three placements; on `public_exposure` (the twenty-three were blown in m05) her handlers and the forty-seven; on `turn_double_agent` the names Torres couldn't give. The deck also holds Tomb Alpha's tapes, his recorded teaching at the first workshop with annotation sheets in his hand (he gave the same lecture on SAFETYNET's course and at Tomb Alpha, which is why HaX knows it; stage 6 keeps this, milestone MS-20) ("He teaches what he'll do. If he built the bunker the way he taught it, it's on there."; m09 change 16). The offer and the second purge choice are one decision (§6.1), made **inside the call**. **Her price is the whole deck** (m09 MC §3.2, ch. 1): take the name and both her file and the Alpha tapes go; refuse, and HaX saves one tape. There is no "pull nothing" button. Every cost is said aloud on the call before the answer, with no pronoun for WREN before the name: "One of your four is mine. Was mine. Your Architect took my placement for Montana and didn't ask. I'll give you the name tonight. The price is the deck. All of it goes." / "Say no and I wipe it myself. Same deck, no name." Her threat, said before the choice, covers the deal only: "I can't see which tape you pull. I can see that you pulled one. Pull anything after you've had the name, and I'll ring my placement and tell them they've been sold. You'll have bought nothing, and half my people will have moved by morning." HaX's boast, "I can beat that wipe.", gets "Nobody has.", which is why a refusing Recruiter drops off sure her file is gone; and HaX prices the invoice as a moral cost: "Buy from her and SAFETYNET goes in an ENTROPY ledger... He'll read the sale, and he'll quote it at you." The call's first line sets `recruiter_offer = "refused"` with `deck_pulled = "alpha_tapes"` and `recruiter_secret` by branch; every button writes a legal pair; its done-flag `deck_choice_made` is set on its last choice; a closed call rings again on leaving the room and resumes at the last unanswered choice, with two resume points after a "yes" (§6.1). The call is voiced (F17; under F20 only the call's own knots, m09 S4 §6.1). **Take** (`taken`, `deck_pulled = none`): she names Halina Kowalczyk (`sleeper_named`, set on her naming line, never on the button), hangs up and wipes the deck; both tapes go; she walks free into a later season. When the player first enters Sorensen's office, the drop terminal's ledger books the sale (HaX's mapping on entry; moved inside the office so the seam between Acts 2 and 3 stays quiet, m09 RL §10 item 18) ("Sale. WREN. To Agent 0x00. Consideration, one deck. Booked."): **the invoice**, which m10's Tesseract reads (m09 change 30). **Double-cross** (take the name, then "[Pull her file anyway.]", priced by HaX first: "Pull her file and we get half her people. She warns the rest, and Kowalczyk runs."; `double_crossed`, `her_file`): the file and the name, but the name's use is void. She rings WREN aboard the aircraft; her unvoiced text reaches the player on first entering the tape room ("You took the name and something off my deck. I said I'd know before your plane landed. It's still in the air."); Kowalczyk walks off the apron at Helena on landing and is gone; about half the network is rolled up ("The Recruiter's network: about half of it. She warned the rest.", m10 S6 §3.5), and in m10 WREN waits at the core beside Tesseract (§4.4). **Refuse** ("Then I'll tidy my own desk. Gone. Goodnight, agent."; HaX: "She watched the counter. Not the copy. One of them. Now."): HaX pulls her file (`named`: her whole network is rolled up) or the Alpha tapes (`refused`: she stays at large, and the tapes play, below), and the player does the cross-reference the long way. A hang-up gets the same tape choice with no Recruiter line ("She's wiping it. She thinks it's gone."). Refusing earns a clean record that m10 pays once. **The Alpha tapes play** (user decision F12, 2026-10-21): on `alpha_tapes`, after the identity scene, one voiced clip of about fifteen seconds at the tape room's reel deck (the deck opens the clip; Tomb Beta's station is in the salvage cage next door, m09 RL §3.9), his m07 voice treated as an old lecture-room recording ("The best lock there is opens for the right name. Nobody fights a door that's expecting them." / "And build your own way out first. Always. Then let them build their way in."; HaX: "Tape two. He gave that lecture every year."), built as an ink conversation voiced by TTS with a recording style (transcript fallback), and the sheets decode to his first draft of the line (row 120). In m10 they pay three times (§3.2, §4.4). What else she says depends on #61–62. | H | none for presence; m09 exports `recruiter_offer`, `deck_pulled`, `sleeper_named` |

### 2.9 Weight check

High-weight payoffs per mission, to show the load is shared:
- m09: the identity reveal (#70, #97, #98, #106, #125), the Reading (#101), the Recruiter and her offer, priced at the whole deck (#60–61), the ledger and WREN (#63, #117), Nightshade's file (#104–106), and the notice every player decides (§1.3, #48).
- m10, the five full scenes: Nightshade at the door (#104–105), Victoria (#32–33), Voltage (#44–45), Ghost (#13–16), Gary (#23). Plus disclosure (#20, #48), the m07 team (#81–83), the whodunit (#117), the line (#120).




## 3. Imports and exports

Mechanism and rules: `README_scenario_design.md` "Carrying choices between missions" and `docs/agents/CARRY_OVER_PLAN.md` §2. Every alias below is the default `mNN_<var>` form, so `as` can be left out. Every default matches the source's `globalVariables` default (checked against each erb on 2026-10-20: every ✱ name below exists there; the two ✱✱ names do not yet).

**The rule for both missions.** No import appears in `concludeRequires` or an `unlockCondition` (the validator errors on it anyway). Each import is one of five kinds, given in the **Use** column:

- **R** route: opens an extra way through. The way without it still works.
- **H** helper: a person or a fact that makes something quicker or clearer.
- **O** obstacle: makes something harder, and the same mission always offers a way round it.
- **P** presence: decides who is standing in a room. Every such room has a default occupant (§5.3), so the room works with the import at its default.
- **L** line: changes what someone says, or what a document reads.
- **L+** a line that sets the whole form of a scene (which version of the identity scene, the briefing or the Recruiter's call plays). Built and tested with Tier 1, because a wrong branch breaks a scene, not a sentence.

Every mission also imports `completed` for each source (`m01_completed` … `m08_completed`; m10 adds `m09_completed`). Ink uses it to tell "never played" from "played and chose the default". A player who starts at m09 gets the default line everywhere, and the default line must stand on its own (it is new writing, so there is no older audio to preserve). **Wherever a present, at-large or penalising value equals the import's default** (`m07_mercer_fate == ""`, `m02_reeves_fate == ""`, `m07_team_assignment == ""`, every m08 Vector/Phantom value, the m01 clean-record line), the condition also requires that source's `completed`, so a player who never played it meets the default and is told nothing false about a mission they never had. The rule covers m09's exports in m10 too (stage 8, ruling 4, from SA M2): `m09_nightshade_shown_file` defaults to `false`, and `false` is the penalising "not shown" value, so m10's not-shown obstacle version (his map with the tripwire, his channel still live) is gated on `m09_completed`. An m08 triple-agent save that skipped m09 gets the neutral version of the door scene, with no tripwire penalty for a choice m09 never offered.

**Never-played canon.** What a player who skipped a mission is treated as having lived through. Each wall card's never-played sentence (row 57) states it, and it matches the default occupant in §5.3:

| Source | Never-played canon | What it means in m09/m10 |
|---|---|---|
| m01 | Derek Lawson was arrested and said nothing. | Arrest line in his case; no innocents entry either way. |
| m02 | Ghost was never caught; the hospital recovered. | Ghost on the network as a stranger; the workshop's default technician, Imran Siddiqui, with his never-played grievance (a board that never read its emails); no Reeves. |
| m03 | Zero Day's UK broker was never identified, and Zero Day kept trading. (m03 is in Cardiff; milestone MS-01.) | Pieter Vos in the gallery. |
| m04 | Voltage disappeared after Albion. | Wendell Briggs, the power-room technician, at the switchgear; HaX in the phone slot. |
| m05 | Torres was arrested. | His ledger entry reads "lost". |
| m06 | The fund was found, then lost. | Full staffing at Tomb Gamma. |
| m07 | One team, four sites. Austin was reached in time; two of ours died at the others. (Matches m08's own never-played line, "one team to send".) | No dispatch line, no backdoor channel. Social Fabric's story stays a held draft (the server-hall prop; the media room is cut). The closing card's cell lines read as if Austin was covered and the other sites were not. |
| m08 | The mole is in custody and saying nothing. | Nightshade in the van at the forward post: "Door.", with Netherton's tether decision (row 107). On a cold start of m10 with `not m08_completed`, Netherton says who he is first (§4.0). |
| m09 (for m10) | The name proved, the hour found, Tomb Gamma's defences on paper, in two documents (ruling 6, from stage 8 SR major 1): Helena's plans, the 1962 construction drawings from the lease file ("Refit drawings: withdrawn by lessee.", BP F-02), which every player holds and can check Nightshade's payment against; and, from flag 4, Tesseract's own list of the database entries he built the defences from, which names them without placing them. One of the team is WREN. | m10's cold-start briefing (§4.0): the name and the hour in front of the team, WREN at the private aside; no m09 callbacks (confession answer, Reading, Recruiter, Albion notice), and HaX reads the unsent notice off his box at flag 4. On the m08 triple-agent branch Nightshade's door scene plays its neutral version, not "not shown" (ruling 4). |

Imports are read-only: one shared ink include per mission (`m09_carryover_vars.ink`, `m10_carryover_vars.ink`) declares them all as `VAR`, and nothing assigns them.

**How R and P imports are built.** Imports are not available to the ERB (CARRY_OVER_PLAN §2.5), so a presence or route is a `game_loaded` eventMapping that shows or hides an NPC with `setVisible` (never `#remove_npc`), or opens a door or gives an item. Each one raises a validator warning by design (§2.6). Stage 8 expects one warning per R/P import and signs each off.

**Tiers** (round 1, D-07; boundary moved in round 2, R2-09). The user wants as many decisions as possible to carry, so the list stays broad, but not every import gets the same build effort:

- **Tier 1**: every import that puts a person, a prop, a helper, a route or an obstacle in a room (Use R, H, O, P), whatever its weight. Built first, and every condition is covered by at least one named route-driver profile at stage 8 (Mercer in the server hall gets a profile of its own).
- **Tier 2**: lines only (Use L): spoken lines, wall cards, document text, talk-down options and epilogue lines, written as fixed strings and said *to* the player at a moment. Checked by the dialogue lint and a read-through of each branch.
- **Tier 3**: cut, each with a reason (listed under each table). Cut names are withdrawn from §3.3's export additions.

Every R or H import has a HaX or NPC line that names the player's earlier choice at the moment it helps.

Unchanged by stages 1–2; stage 3 added `m04_crew_lost` (already in m04's `exportGlobals`, so no export addition). `m04_disclosure_choice` also chooses HaX's notice lines; the m04 Voltage pair no longer does (stage 7, m09 R2 audio cut 1 merged the watch and the send into one line, "Our watch sits on that kit. Send it, the vendor starts a fix, and the watch comes down.", for every branch; the "Voltage's people" / "what's left of his crew" naming now lives only in m10, in Netherton's database list), and `m01_maya_ko` makes the letters give back the warning Maya's names would have given (m09 MC §6). The deed to Tomb Gamma is a task for every player (it is also the paper half of the defence plans), so the no-coordinate route changes the briefing and HaX's emphasis, never what the player must do.

Task Force Null's wall in the briefing (row 57) is one object per earlier mission. Each card has a played version on the imports above and a never-played version that states the canon in one sentence.

**Tier 3, cut:** `m04_casualties_occurred`. Without `crew_lost` it means the bank vented into an empty hall, and there is nothing left for Tesseract to argue with. (It was already exported, so no export change.)

**91 variables (Tier 1 40, Tier 2 51) plus nine `completed` imports**, after stage 8 added `m09_alpha_annotations_read` (ruling 5); 90 after the stages 4–5 fold. The history follows. Stage 0 ended at 63. Stages 1–2 added `m09_sleeper_shortlist` (Tier 1) and `m09_deck_pulled` (then Tier 2), making 65. Stage 3 (m10 MC P8, m09 MC ch. 2) moved `m09_deck_pulled` and `m09_recruiter_offer` up to Tier 1 (a helper and a presence) and added `m09_cage_bin` and `m09_legacy_files_saved` to Tier 2: Tier 1 38 → 40, Tier 2 27 → 25 → 27, total 67. **The milestone fixes add eighteen Tier 2 lines-only imports** (seventeen for the knock-out pattern, F16, §3.6, and `m08_named_on_evidence`, M-12): Tier 1 40, Tier 2 45, total **85**, plus nine `completed`. None changes a room, so the Tier 1 build and its route-driver profiles are unchanged; the eighteen are read in one place, the shared include's `ko_pattern()` function and the aside, and are checked by one table-driven test of that function (§3.6). **The stages 4–5 fold adds five more Tier 2 names**, m02's staff knock-out latches (`m02_receptionist_ko`, `m02_ward_nurse_ko`, `m02_raval_ko`, `m02_gary_ko`, `m02_dr_kim_ko`; orchestrator, §3.6): Tier 2 45 → 50, total **90**, read only through `ko_pattern()` like the rest. Victoria's KO branch, Voltage's capture and the rest of the "absent" values put the room's default occupant in place (§5.3).

**Milestone additions** (F16, M-12; 2026-10-21): fifteen more names, every one already a key in its mission's `globalVariables` (checked in each erb, read-only), so all are JSON-only (✱) under F4 and none needs an ink change (no ✱✱). m10 reads them; m09 reads none. `m03_danny_ko`, `m04_robert_vance_ko`, `m08_netherton_ko` and `m08_hax_ko` are already exported or already on this list. Total **53** names (38 + 15).

**Stages 4–5 fold** (orchestrator, 2026-10-21): m02's five staff knock-out latches join the knock-out pattern (§3.6). Each is already a `globalVarOnKO` key in m02's `globalVariables` (`scenarios/m02_ransomed_trust/scenario.json.erb`, read-only: `receptionist_ko` Bernie Nwosu, `ward_nurse_ko` Sister Doyle, `raval_ko` Nurse Raval, `gary_ko` Gary Whitlock, `dr_kim_ko` Dr Sarah Kim; m02's credits list the same five), so all five are JSON-only (✱). m10 reads them; m09 does not. Total **58** names (53 + 5).

Before them there were 38 names across eight missions (round 1 withdrew `halloran_accused`, `named_on_evidence` and `suspect_theory`, Tier 3). Unchanged by stage 3: neither stage 3 document adds an export to m01–m08. m09's new import `m04_crew_lost` is already in m04's `exportGlobals` (m10 has read it since stage 0), and the m05 pair m09 stage 3 leans on (`recruiter_deal_confessed`, `found_pipeline_list`) was already in the m05 row. After each addition, run the validator on the source (export names must stay stable from then on, CARRY_OVER_PLAN 2.9). JSON-only additions work for runs already finished, because a run's snapshot holds every one of its own globals.

**Ink changes, approved by the user (F5, 2026-10-20).** Neither changes a spoken line, so no audio is regenerated.

1. **m02 `reeves_fate`.** Add a string global (default `""`) set once in `m02_closing_debrief.ink` where the debrief already routes (`:646-668`). Values: `arrested`, `ko`, `flagged` (all in custody); `escaped`, `missed` (walked out; at large); `unnoticed` (still on staff; at large for the finale). Public exposure is not a separate route (it rides on arrested, `:677`), so it gets no value. Then export it.
2. **m07 `teacher_discussed`.** Promote the local ink `VAR` to a scenario global (add it to `globalVariables`, default `false`, and a `#set_global:teacher_discussed:true` beside `ink/m07_phone_agent_0x99.ink:777`). Then export it.

**Runs finished before the ink changes** have neither global, so they import `""` / `false`. The fallbacks are therefore the read rule for every existing save, not only a contingency: Reeves reads from `insider_asset_arrested` / `insider_asset_escaped` whenever `m02_reeves_fate == ""` and `m02_completed` (row 22); HaX's reveal for a pre-change m07 run uses the "teacher not discussed" form, with `m06_architect_identity_found` still choosing between the two openings (row 98).

**Twelve exports** in all, plus `m09_completed`: `nightshade_shown_file`, `sleeper_named`, `sleeper_shortlist`, `recruiter_offer`, `deck_pulled`, `albion_notified`, `hax_confession_answer`, `recruiter_secret`, `reading_finished`, `cage_bin`, `legacy_files_saved`, `alpha_annotations_read`. Stages 1–2 added `sleeper_shortlist` and `deck_pulled` (nine); stage 3 added `cage_bin` and `legacy_files_saved` (orchestrator ruling; m09 MC §10); stage 8 added `alpha_annotations_read` (ruling 5; JSON only, since the global already exists in m09).

### 3.5 m10's exports

For a later season; nothing reads them yet. Seven names, each with the one moment it is computed (m10 MC §12, S3-14):

- `ending`: `arrest` / `surrender` / `lethal` (justified) / `executed` (cold; m10 change 20) / `scorched` / `joined`. Set by one pick each (the end-for-him pick, "wipe both", the Join key's confirmation; Tesseract is `koImmune`, so there is no knock-out route, m10 S4-8), always after `database_outcome`. The ending scene completes the conclusion task, so `/conclude` never runs with it empty (m10 MC P34, P48).
- `database_outcome`: `secured` / `disclosed` / `both_wiped` / `published` (on Join).
- `sleeper_caught`: true from the start on `m09_sleeper_named and m09_recruiter_offer = "taken"`; on a right accusation (face to face or on the line); and on the double-cross branch when she is taken at the core. Stopping a free WREN at the core is not an accusation and leaves it as it was. False if never named, including a knocked-out Kowalczyk nobody accused.
- `wrong_accusation`: true on any wrong accusation, and stays true after a recall.
- `head_start_out`: `none` / `zero_day` / `ghost` / `both`, computed at the outer lock's own unlock event, whichever way it was opened.
- `partial_release`: `none` / `small` / `large` / `published` (on Join), set on "Stop it".
- `media_story_ran`: computed when `ending` is set; with the media room cut it is true on `m07_completed` and uncovered Fracture (the story runs from outside, row 84), and on `executed` on every branch (m10 change 40); false on Join. The name is kept for the later season; it no longer refers to a room.

Stage 9 adds them; they cost nothing and stop the next season having to ask for an ink change. The local decision globals (the briefing stance on Quintero, each slot's decision, Ghost's outcome, the console, the tasks, the accusation, the exchanges and derived `points_landed`, `join_step`, `cold_lowered`, the core's choices) and the done-flags are in m10 MC §12 (P20).

### 3.6 The knock-out pattern (F16, milestone D-06)

A player who put unarmed people on the floor mission after mission should hear it from the man who has been watching. Approved by the user as F16 (2026-10-21): KO-pattern exports from m03–m08, with any ink change listed per mission before it is made. At the stages 4–5 fold the orchestrator widened m02's entry to its staff latches, five more JSON-only exports under F4 (row 27).

**What exists in the shipped missions** (read from each `scenario.json.erb`'s `globalVariables` and `exportGlobals`, read-only, 2026-10-21). No mission from m03 to m08 has one global that records "the player knocked out a bystander", and none counts knock-outs (m04's `operatives_defeated` counts hostiles only). Each mission has one boolean latch per knock-out-able NPC, written by the engine through `globalVarOnKO` the moment the NPC goes down, so every latch is already in every finished run's snapshot. A JSON-only export of the latches therefore works for runs finished before the change; a new derived global in the source mission (an eventMapping or ink) would not, and would mean editing a finished mission's behaviour. So the design exports the raw latches and derives the pattern in m10.

| Mission | Bystander latches (all boolean, default `false`) | Already exported? | Change to the finished mission | Who they are; who is left out |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| m01 | `maya_ko`, `kevin_ko`, `sarah_ko` | no; already ✱ in §3.3 | JSON only (already listed) | Viral Dynamics staff. Counts with `m01_completed` |
| m02 | `patient_assaulted`, `receptionist_ko`, `ward_nurse_ko`, `raval_ko`, `gary_ko`, `dr_kim_ko` | no; `patient_assaulted` already ✱ in §3.3 | JSON only: add the five staff latches ✱ (stages 4–5 fold, orchestrator) | A struck patient, and Bernie Nwosu, Sister Doyle, Nurse Raval, Gary Whitlock and Dr Sarah Kim; m02's credits list the five as assaults (`m02 scenario.json.erb:199-202`). Graham Reeves (`night_security_supervisor_ko`) is the insider and doesn't count |
| m03 | `guard_knocked_out`, `receptionist_ko`, `danny_ko` | `danny_ko` yes; the other two no | JSON only: add `guard_knocked_out`, `receptionist_ko` ✱ | Terry Mott, Ffion Rees, Danny Foster; m03's own debrief groups the same set (`m03_closing_debrief.ink:529`). Terry can turn hostile, but only after catching the player at a lock, and m03's credits list his knock-out as a warning, so it counts. `victoria_ko` is the target |
| m04 | `security_guard_ko`, `robert_vance_ko` | no; `robert_vance_ko` already ✱ | JSON only: add `security_guard_ko` ✱ | The site guard ("the one bystander the debrief scolds you for hitting", erb comment) and Vance. The operatives are hostiles |
| m05 | `lisa_ko`, `owen_ko`, `halloran_ko`, `patricia_ko` | no | JSON only: add all four ✱ | Lisa Park, Owen Gallagher, Dr Ruth Halloran, Patricia Morgan; none can turn hostile. `torres_ko` is the target |
| m06 | `guard_ko`, `trader_ko`, `analyst_ko` | no | JSON only: add all three ✱ | The checkpoint guard, Dani Okonkwo, Priya Raghavan; m06's debrief groups the same three (`m06_closing_debrief.ink:387`). Irina, Kemal and Satoshi are not bystanders |
| m07 | `elena_ko` | no | JSON only: add ✱ | Elena Rodriguez, who runs and never attacks (her `#hostile` was removed in pass 2). Hollis is paid and can go for the player, Park and Mercer are hostile, so none counts |
| m08 | `offduty_agent_ko`, `analyst_ko`, `athena_ko`, `netherton_ko`, `hax_ko` | no; `netherton_ko`, `hax_ko` already ✱ | JSON only: add `offduty_agent_ko`, `analyst_ko`, `athena_ko` ✱ | The floor (m08's debrief groups the three, `m08_closing_debrief.ink:125`) and the player's own director and handler. Vector and Phantom are suspects, and their m10 cost already pays (row 112); Nightshade is the mole |

**As shipped at stage 7** (`m10_tesseract.ink` `ko_sting`, run inside exchange 3): the three band lines above, word for word, then on `m08_hax_ko` a fourth, "At the Citadel, it was your own handler." (R3 A3-03, so m08's knock-out of HaX is said to the player's face, not only counted). `m08_hax_ko` is one of the 27 names, so the band line always plays first. m09 pays both m08 knock-outs of SAFETYNET staff in person: HaX's first briefing line and Netherton's pen line at the debrief (§3.1).

**No ink change in any finished mission** (nothing ✱✱): every name is already a key in its mission's `globalVariables`. The additions are one line each in `exportGlobals` (§3.3), made at stage 9 like the rest, with the validator run on each source afterwards.

**Imports into m10.** Twenty-two Tier 2 names (§3.2): F16's seventeen and, from the stages 4–5 fold, m02's five staff latches; plus the three m01 latches, `m02_patient_assaulted` and `m04_robert_vance_ko`, which m10 already imported. `ko_pattern()` reads 27 names in all (m01 3, m02 6, m03 3, m04 2, m05 4, m06 3, m07 1, m08 5). m09 imports none: the Reading's innocents entry stays m01/m02 only, and is capped at four entries.

**The derivation.** One function in `m10_carryover_vars.ink`, `ko_pattern()`, returns how many earlier missions have at least one latch set: m01 on `m01_completed and (m01_maya_ko or m01_kevin_ko or m01_sarah_ko)`; m02 on `m02_patient_assaulted` or any of its five staff latches; m03 on any of its three; m04 on either; m05 on any of four; m06 on any of three; m07 on `m07_elena_ko`; m08 on any of five. A mission the player never played reads all `false` and adds nothing. Nothing in m10 is gated on it (imports never gate). One table-driven test of the function (a handful of seeded saves, run on the F15 bootstrap) replaces a branch read of 27 names.

**How Tesseract's ledger reads it.** A ledger sting, like `m04_crew_lost`, `m02_patient_bed4_deceased` and `m06_satoshi_stance`: it stings first and decides nothing. It plays once, in exchange 3, whose claim is about clean hands: whatever the player picks, he answers with the sting before his concession or his dry reply, so a landed point still lands. Fixed strings by band, no printed number:
- `ko_pattern() == 1`: "You put someone on the floor who wasn't armed. Once. I noticed."
- `2` or `3`: "More than once, you've put someone on the floor who wasn't armed. Clean hands, Agent?"
- `4` or more: "Most of the operations I've watched, you put someone on the floor who wasn't armed. That isn't a lapse. It's a method."

**The clean-record test.** The clean first-day record answer (exchange 1, slot 3) now also needs `ko_pattern() == 0`, so he can never be answered with a clean first day by a player whose record he has just quoted. Its wording is unchanged (it claims the first day only). Stage 7 (m10 group A) added `not m01_player_launched_attack` to the same gate, so a player who pressed the button in m01 is never offered "[My first day, I had a choice to hurt people. I didn't.]".

The contracts and the ink win over this file for in-mission behaviour.

Every m09 and m10 import whose source doesn't export it yet is one of these 60, and each of the 58 is a key in its source's `globalVariables` (m01 3, m02 9 incl. `reeves_fate`, m03 3, m04 7, m05 6, m06 5, m07 7 incl. `teacher_discussed`, m08 20) | |

Voltage got away.", covers it, and the notice's watch naming was cut at R2) and `m07_team_redirected` (the redirect also writes `trojan_horse`, which the wall reads). With `m06_assets_seized` added for the briefing's seized arm (BP F-03), 44 → 43.

I've never been so pleased to fill in a form.", m10 SE) was in no ink and no list at stage 7. Stage 8 keeps the import and writes it as a HaX text in `scenarios/m10_full_disclosure/texts/BARKS_AND_TEXTS.md` (rulings 14, 15).
