`door_log_given` (m10, bool) predates stage 7 but changed meaning at R2: it now gates HaX's "[Who was where at ten past?]" topic, so **stage 9 must set it from the door-log bark's mapping** as well as from her "[The door log.]" topic, or a player who only heard the bark never sees the alibi topic (m10 contract §9). `lock_guard_known` is written with `~` by Ghost, Victoria and Vos (not OT's `#set_global`).

**Locals that cross files: none.** Every m10 file-local carries its file's prefix and appears in one file only. m09's said-once guards are file-local too: `said_floor_line` (the debrief), `confession_heard`, `hand_answered_here`, `notice_sent_here`, `nightshade_sent_here`, `keypad_topic_asked` (HaX's phone), `recruiter_hung_up` (the Recruiter), `noor_asked_peg` and Noor's others; `hub_quiet` is declared separately in five person files (Noor, Old Coyote, Tran, Sorensen's hatch and cage) and must never become a scenario global, or the five would share one value.

## 4. m10 ending matrix

Six endings: four successes (arrest, surrender and cooperation, justified lethal force, and cold lethal force, the darkest), Scorched Earth, and Join ENTROPY. User decision F11 split lethal force in two, each signposted (m10 change 20); user decision F13 makes surrender a deal with no public trial and arrest a public trial with no cells (m10 MC P4). All six come after the same four VM flags, so `concludeRequires` is the same for every ending (the technical work only) and the ending is a global the debrief and credits read. The conclusion aim's own task is completed by the ending scene, which plays only once `ending` is set (m10 MC P34). m10 MC §6–§8 is the full specification of the core; this section is the summary both missions share.

### 4.0 The opening and the route

**Start kit** (every branch; inventory doesn't carry between missions): the player's usual kit, including the CyberChef workstation (`type: "workstation"`, as m08 and m09; the margin note's decode needs it and no room supplies one; milestone S4–5 F-02), plus Kowalczyk's PIN cracker (`kowalczyk_cracker`), its description saying who gave it: "handed it to you before Helena", quoting m09's line whole on m09 saves and dropping "never been near him" only on a cold start with m08 unplayed (m10 change 26; m10 S6-08, S6-31). On a cold start (`not m09_completed`) she hands it over at m10's own briefing (`m10_director_netherton.ink`), so the description needs a cold-start variant without "before Helena" (stage 8 SA M4; owner the m10 contract).

**Briefing** (a cutscene at the forward post, about four minutes; Netherton runs it in person, HaX beside him all night). Played m09: WREN detained on screen if m09 took her name (`taken`: Quintero cuffs her against the van; Arakawa then breaches alone, or Quintero if Arakawa takes silent Nightshade to the blast door); on `double_crossed`, an empty place at the table and Netherton's one line, "Kowalczyk walked off the apron in Helena. We know where she has gone.", with the same breaching rule (m10 MC P31); otherwise the kit check, with the breaching case on the table, opened at 01:52 (it comes out shut; m10 S6-21), and its sign-out sheet ("Opened: 01:52. Kowalczyk / Arakawa."; "Opened: not yet." while the clock reads 01:45). At the road end lies **Citadel Incident File V-47** (`incident_file`), the raid's copy of the Director's file on the theft: the catalogue taken, 92,460 entries, with the vault's personnel index (1,206 staff records); the door into the vault left open under watch, to follow the copy, on Netherton's signature ("3. The door. Left open, under watch, to follow the copy. Risk accepted, on my signature."; F7, D-13). It does not say the watch reached the coordinate: on m08's knock-out-before-fate branch it didn't, and m09's deed found the bunker (BP F-09); and the stores half of the cracker canon (row 29). It stops there: that the line is still open and reaches the master is first read in his file index at the core and said by HaX at flag 4 (m10 S6 §2.5, S6-01). A captured Voltage is visible in the second van, the only helper the briefing shows. The stakes give both modes of the Publication and both lines of his sheet (§1.2), and Netherton draws the line between full disclosure and the Publication, once (§1.2, milestone D-02). **Quintero objects** to going in tonight, for the second time in two days ("For the record, I'd go in at noon tomorrow with twice the people."; Netherton: "Noted, Agent Quintero. Twice now. The record also says six o'clock.", with "Twice now" only on `m09_completed`, since it points at her Helena objection); the player backs her ("[She's right. And we're going anyway.]"), backs Netherton ("[The record says six. We go.]") or says nothing, a stance she answers at the outer lock and again at 06:00 (m10 change 15; m10 MC C1). Netherton's handbook line goes here: the chapter on raids by invitation is one page long, and it says don't. The briefing's question hub lives in HaX's phone, not the cutscene, which keeps the cutscene to its spine and saves about a minute (m10 S4-6). Vector and Phantom are at the table or declined, by branch. Nothing about WREN is said in front of the team. **The private aside** (m10 change 35): Netherton and HaX take the player to the van end, the team in view and out of earshot. One of this team, "placed eleven years ago"; he keeps them together because benching four agents tells WREN they know; he will say nothing to them, "and neither will you", and then the cost of not looking: "Whoever it is wants the same room you do. Find them before you get there." (m10 MC P16). On `m09_sleeper_shortlist and not m09_sleeper_named`, HaX: "Two faces from Helena. Watch both." On `m08_named_on_evidence` with the whodunit running, HaX: "You caught the last one on paper. Do it again." (M-12). Named form on `m09_recruiter_offer == "taken"`, never on `m09_sleeper_named` alone, which is also true on the double-cross (milestone MS-03; check `double_crossed` first, as m09 SA §7.1 does): "She was the one the ledger meant. If there is a second, it doesn't show one. Watch anyway." On `taken` the aside ends at the van window, Kowalczyk cuffed inside: she asks the player "When did you know?" here, the only time they meet her after the reveal, and her reply to their answer comes at 06:00 as on the other branches (milestone M-07). **The taken answers** (stage 7 R3 B3-01, the same in Netherton's aside and Kowalczyk's `van`): "[In Helena. She sold me your name.]" (value `bench`; it replaced "[In Helena. Your name on the placing sheet.]", which could be false: on `taken` the Recruiter gives the name, and the placing sheet is optional and never names her) and "[Before Helena. I had a feeling about you.]" (`briefing`); her 06:00 reply to the first is "Eleven years she paid my mum's care home. Then she sold me to you.". Asked "[Do we know who?]" at the aside, Netherton: "No. And if I bench four agents to find out, WREN knows we know." On `named`, Netherton adds why her network is still at its desks, and WREN unwarned: "The arrests start at six, and no sooner. WREN would hear." (D-01; stage 7 wording). **The door scene** follows the aside (§2 rows 104–107): every branch ends on a closing choice at the table that sets `door_scene_done`, and the breach waits on flag 1, that flag and the pair back. **Never played m09** (R2-11): the same scene carries the three facts m09 would have given, in about 45 extra seconds. On `not m08_completed`, Netherton first says who Nightshade is. The name (Dr Adrian Tesseract, proved in Helena from his own hand, with HaX's one sentence: SAFETYNET's former chief strategist, who trained her and half the field, and walked out seven years ago) and the hour go in front of the team; the third fact, WREN, goes at the private aside. Kowalczyk hands over the cracker here ("This one's mine. Never been through stores, never been near him.", dropping the last clause on `not m08_completed`). Stage 2 reads this path with no other mission in mind.

**The night in UTC** (the Citadel's clock; in Montana it is the evening of day 12, on the Citadel's clock the early hours of day 13). The forward post is one room with two ends: the vans and the table at the road end, where Netherton, HaX and Malhotra stay all night, and the gate and its terminal at the other. From 01:45: the kit check; the breaching case comes out shut, is opened at 01:52, and stays open on the table until the split; at 01:58 WREN copies Malhotra's badge, which lies by his headset, with a cloner from the case. **The pre-breach split** (R3-03), while the player works flag 1 at the gate terminal, so Arakawa's beat fills VM time (m10 change 16): Quintero and Kowalczyk recon the service tunnel, which forks, and both branches come out by the core's service door, so the fork decides nothing (m10 change 11). Arakawa holds the gate beside the player and, at 02:10 on the countdown object, makes a call off the net to his daughter in Billings; Malhotra, from the van end, logs it aloud ("Arakawa, you're off the net."), which puts both men where someone could see them. The pair come back about 02:20 (Quintero: "We split at the fork. I took the drain. It comes up by the service door anyway."; Kowalczyk: "Door end. Service door's real. Steel, a reader, a camera that doesn't move."). About 02:30: the breach. Netherton is on the net (`breach_call`) and stays at the table with Vector and Phantom where present; there is no gate copy of him (milestone A16; m10 OT §6). The countdown objects then move on story beats (§4.2). **Every step here is an event, never a timer** (m10 S4-7, OT §6): the split starts on the second of the table's done-flag (`door_scene_done`) and the gate console's first use, so it never lands inside Nightshade's scene and always after the 01:58 copy (which closes MS-23's timing half); Arakawa's call follows when Netherton's split line closes; the pair come back on the second of his call and flag 1; and the breach waits on the table, flag 1 and the pair back (§4.0 "Forced scenes and nets"). HaX asks the player to start the gate console before the table, so the VM boots during the door scene.

**Where the raid team stands after the breach** (R2-22). The engine has no friendly follow or escort, so companions are staged per room (N-14): each is placed (`setVisible`) in the room where they meet the player, with one line that covers the move. Who stands in which room, on the default branch and on each variant (taken, double-cross, tether, `wren_warned`, `officer_out`, each knock-out), is m10 RL's roster table, room by room, with each copy's id (milestone S4–5 A6; m10 RL §2.9): `kowalczyk_`, `arakawa_` and `quintero_` copies with the suffix `_so`, `_ws`, `_pr` and `_hall` from the security office to the server hall; `quintero_lock`, `minder_lock` and `nightshade_lock` at the outer lock; `quintero_core`; and at the forward post the briefing-table copies `kowalczyk_post`, `quintero_post` and `arakawa_post`, which are `koImmune` like Malhotra, while Arakawa's gate copy `arakawa_gate` stays knockable (m10 S6-26; a `kowalczyk_ko` therefore always happens below ground). This paragraph is the summary. Quintero is placed in each main-path room as the player enters it, except the gallery; she catches up at the outer lock by the service side and **holds the lock** while Tesseract talks to 0x00 alone ("He wants you on your own. I'll be on this side of it."), coming in at the noise if WREN does (m10 change 30). A taken-in Nightshade holds it with her; with Quintero out, a minder from the van team stands in (m10 MC §3.1). Malhotra runs comms from the van. Arakawa and Kowalczyk are placed one room ahead, Kowalczyk breaching, which is why her badge turns up everywhere; on the silent-Nightshade tether branch Arakawa instead holds the blast door with Nightshade all night, and Kowalczyk breaches ahead alone and goes ahead from the security office, a room earlier. Kowalczyk otherwise peels off in the server hall ("I'll take the service side and meet you at the lock."), and Arakawa has one line about it ("Service side. She always takes the door nobody's watching."), or Quintero if Arakawa is at the blast door. With Kowalczyk off the team (`m09_sleeper_named`, taken or double-crossed) and Arakawa on the tether, Quintero breaches ahead alone, so she is not at the outer lock (a minder holds it) and reaches the core late (m10 MC P47). The player accuses face to face when the accused is in the room and **on the line** otherwise (m10 change 3). The raid net speaks through HaX's channel and counts as hers for the one-voice rule (Netherton, Malhotra, any accused or recalled officer).

**Why the readers log SAFETYNET badges.** The theft took the vault's personnel index along with the database (a vulnerability catalogue holds no staff credentials; milestone M-04, said once, by HaX), and Tesseract loaded SAFETYNET's own credentials from it, so the bunker's readers accept them; the blast door knows 0x00 the same way, and opens slowly for them by name (unless silent Nightshade's crank opens it). The readers hold 1,206 SAFETYNET badges; the service door takes any of them, the blast door one by name (the alarm log; m10 S6-14). At 02:10, at the door end of the recon, WREN used the copy of Malhotra's badge on the core's service door and propped it: Tesseract's way out (Scorched Earth uses it) and, if 0x00 won't press the key, her own way back to the key (§4.4; m09 change 8, m10 change 27). The service door's camera is a dummy, a box on a bracket the refit never wired (m10 change 36). No 02:10 entry exists on the named-in-m09 branch. On `double_crossed` the door log shows Kowalczyk's own badge at the service door at 21:47 the evening before: she came in openly (m10 S6-14).

**The route.** Forward post (flag 1; the breach opens on flag 1, `door_scene_done` **and** `pair_back`) → blast door, keyed to 0x00 → tunnel → security office (Ghost's call on the duty handset; the stair down opens on `ghost_call_done`) → workshop floor → power room → server hall (4×2, the act's centre: flag 2 at the release console, flag 3 at the sealed section's own hatch station at the hall's east end; the disarm is armed here; the head start is found here, and the console can kill one share or trace Zero Day's; the gallery stair opens only once flags 2 and 3 are in **and** the gallery's blast shutter is cut, so nobody reaches the lock without that chance or the core without the disarm; m10 change 19, m10 MC P29, m10 S4-11, S4-13, S5-9, OT §4.4) → gallery (a straight walkway, the stair at its west end and the outer lock at its east end; the media room is cut, D-05) → outer lock (every live share goes as it opens) → core (east of the gallery; flag 4; m10 S5-16). Eight rooms in one corridor, every door east–west or between rooms of equal width, so every pair aligns (m10 RL §1). The power room is on the main path (R2-13): the gallery's blast shutter runs off it, and the player has it cut at the switchgear, by whoever holds the power room's slot (§5.3) or by hand. HaX points the way after flag 1: "Whoever's in the power room runs your lights." **The outer lock is a PIN lock with five ways through** (code `6083`, the shift code: the same four digits on the code card, Vos's copy, Ghost's note and the plate's four worn keys; m10 S6 §2.1) (m10 MC §3.5, P12; m10 S4-3, S5-2, S5-12, S5-17, S5-18; OT §4.5), each paying differently: (1) the PIN cracker while the lock guard's cone is off the keypad: his challenge is scoped to the keypad and his cone is drawn, so the risk is visible, and a crack inside the cone brings his challenge as the cracker closes (talk past him once, or he turns hostile with the door open behind the player); (2) the fingerprint kit on the keypad's plate, which shows the four worn keys and makes the crack quick, and needs nobody; (3) the code card, the quiet way, from the lock guard, **Sione Fonoti** (m10 S6 §2.4), once the player knows he carries it (named by escaped Victoria, the gallery rota in the security office's records cupboard, Ghost's card gift or Vos's deal; every route that names him sets `lock_guard_known`, and his ink has an option for each; milestone S4–5 A3), or Vos's own copy on his deal; (4) opened from inside by a Victoria who holds or a Vos who stands aside; (5) Ghost's maintenance code, typed from the note. On the first try at the keypad a bark points at the card, since a PIN lock emits no failure event. The old wording ("Shift code. The duty guard carries it.") is retired: `duty_guard` is the security office's patrol, and the bark was the lock guard's own, so he pointed at himself. The bark stays on the lock guard, Fonoti, reworded to first person so he no longer names himself: "That takes the shift code. It's on my card, and it stays there." (m10 OT §4.5, milestone S4–5 A13; stage 8 m10-R m-3). It is gated on `!globalVars.fonoti_ko`, so a man on the floor doesn't speak. A taken-in Nightshade is not a way through: he holds the lock behind the player. In player-facing text "the plant" is never used for the room, and the sleeper is "WREN" or "the sleeper", never "the plant" (R3-10). **At most one non-HaX voice on the line per room** (R3-01); people in the room speak in person and don't count (m10 MC §0, M-08).

**Forced scenes and nets** (m10 MC rule 1.10, P17; m10 S4-1, S4-18, S5-8; OT §4). The four story doors (the blast door, the stair, the tunnel's inner door, the gallery stair) are RFID locks keyed to a card that exists nowhere, so no pick, cracker or cloner opens them. Each opens by HaX's `unlockDoor` mapping (F19) when its condition on local done-flags holds (the blast door on flag 1, `door_scene_done` and `pair_back`; the stair on `ghost_call_done`; the gallery stair on flags 2 and 3 and `gallery_shutter_cut`; the tunnel's inner door on `room_entered:blast_tunnel` with `!tripwire_armed`, or on `ladder_used` on the tripwire route; m10 OT §4.1, §4.3), never on an import, with a `game_loaded` catch-up and a retry on a failed try at the door; the door's own done-flag is set from the `door_unlocked_by_npc` event that HaX's unlock emits, so nothing claims a door the server refused, and HaX's holding bark names whatever is still missing. **KO immunity landed** (F14, milestone D-08; `koImmune`, committed in 859da19d): every holder of a forced face-to-face scene is `koImmune` (Tesseract, Netherton, HaX in person, Malhotra, Nightshade's copies, a captured Voltage, Kowalczyk's cuffed and core copies, and the briefing-table copies of Kowalczyk, Quintero and Arakawa, m10 S6-26), so the `npc_ko` safety nets on those scenes are dropped (m10 RL §2, S5-8). Knockable slot-holders (Imran, Gary, Wendell, an escaped Voltage, Victoria, Vos, the lock guard) keep their `npc_ko` mappings, which write the slot's outcome, and each has a way past that needs no talk (m10 RL §3).

### 4.1 The core sequence

The core is one room (D-16): the core host terminal on one side, Tesseract seated at the release console on the other. He has been expecting Agent 0x00 since the blast door opened for them by name (row 119), because he needs a SAFETYNET hand on the key (§1.2).

He is non-hostile; his hand rests beside the key. **He is `koImmune` for the whole core scene** (m10 S4-8, RL §6 item 36; F14's key, committed in 859da19d): the engine can't switch the key on or off mid-scene and nothing needs it to, so he can't be knocked out, and the knock-out fallback that stood here (the hand disarm, the database menu at the host terminal, a one-option "[Cuff him.]") is withdrawn. Every ending is reached through his conversation. **The core resumes** (P34): its menus write their globals on the pick (`exchange_1..3`, `console_choice`, `wren_at_core`, `database_outcome`, `ending`), and a walk-away or reload re-enters the first unset one. His conversation always enters at one router knot that reads those globals, because a person-chat restarts after a reload and a reload respawns the player at the forward post, so the walk back ends here (m10 OT §4.6).

0. **Arrival.** His first line, as the player walks in, says what went at the outer lock, one of four fixed strings by `head_start_out` ("Two cells have it. Much good may it do them." / "Zero Day has it. You didn't stop that one." / "Someone's buyers have it. Not Zero Day's." / "Nothing went at four. You were thorough."), because HaX is jammed in the gallery and the panel is easy to miss (m10 S4-12, OT §4.5). He doesn't pause anything, and says why there is no hurry (m10 change 37): "Take your time. It goes at six whatever we say. Or now, if someone presses this. I'd rather it was you." The core's countdown object holds the last beat's time; nothing moves while they talk. HaX comes back on the open line inside his conversation, drawn as its second speaker, so nothing on entry cuts his first line (m10 S4-22, OT §4.6): her one word, the player's designation, and her own line on the release by the same value. He greets her, "Ninety-nine. You've done well with them."; she refused the name on his m09 call ("It's HaX. It has been for seven years."), so here she answers "Still HaX." on every branch (m09 change 13, m10 change 17). Then, to the player: "Get on his box. He'll keep." The player takes flag 4 at the core host terminal; he is an NPC the player approaches. He has three idle lines at most, written to be good rather than many, on events the game sees (first use of the host terminal, a field-guide hint request, a failed submission), never on time, and stage 8 times flag 4 with him in the room (milestone M-13), and HaX's field-guide offer keeps working. As flag 4 lands, HaX reads off his box, as the second speaker inside his conversation (settled at the m10 milestone fixes: `core_router` step 4, opened by his mapping on `flag4_submitted`; no phone call, since opening a call ends an open person-chat; milestone S4–5 ruling, A7; m10 OT §13). The core's back wall carries his file index (`core_files`, readable after flag 4: his workshops, the key's rule, the purge, his notice), and her lines confirm what it shows (m10 S6-03). Her lines: on `not m09_completed`, two lines carrying the fact exchange 2 rests on (he argued for the vendor nine years ago and lost; he wrote the notice and never sent it); on an m09 save, "The notice you read in Helena is on here too." In the same beat, on every branch, her one fact about the console, which is the signposting for the justified-lethal moment: "I've got his box. If anything goes out from that console, I can try to kill the feed. Try."; and the purge warning: "And the line into our vault is open from here. We left it open." / "He's rigged this room. If both copies die, it purges: doors, lights, all of it. He'll be ready for that. We won't." She says it again, shorter, at the database menu (m10 MC §6.1, P5). Asked about the head start afterwards: released, "Two hours for people who'll waste it. They were always scenery."; stopped, "Good. I never liked paying them." (§13 item 19). (Flags 2 and 3 were taken earlier in the server hall, flag 3 at the sealed section's own hatch station: R2-02, m10 S4-13.)
1. **His case** (gated on all four flags, m10 change 43 and m10 S4-11: walking to him before it gets only "Finish on my box first. I'll keep."; the gallery already needs flags 2 and 3, so the missing one can only be flag 4). A conversation, not a fight. First the Publication, plainly, in six lines at most: the hour, the fallback city by city, his figure from memory, why it ends the arms race. Then **the offer, which every player hears** (D-21): it has to be one of them; if he presses it, it's a crime, and if they press it, it's a confession; and if nobody does, "Either you press it, or I get a courtroom. I've costed both." (M-10, §1.2). Then his evidence, opening on his m07 handset line turned round, "I told you once I prefer to watch. I've watched.", with at most three lines from the player's record, by priority: m04 `disclosure_choice`, then m01 `final_choice`, then m07 `debrief_stance` (D-08), each with a default, two at most before the player can answer; it closes "Since your first day." He never claims to have read the player's file. **Then the Recruiter beat** (m10 MC §6.2, P7), which gathers m09's two Recruiter exports in one place after his evidence, so D-08's cap of three stays: (1) `m09_recruiter_secret`, to HaX on the open line: `kept`, his leverage and her "I'll deal with that when this is done."; `told` or `confessed`, she cuts him off at "Forty-seven names": "They told me. Next."; `denied`, he gets the receipt out whole and she hears the lie exposed in front of him: "You told me she was fishing.", and doesn't look at the player again until the database menu; `""`, nothing. (2) `m09_recruiter_offer`, to the player: `taken` or `double_crossed`, the invoice, one ledger sting that decides nothing, "You bought WREN from my Recruiter. You understand costing." (on `double_crossed` with a glance at the service door she came in by; MS-16); `named` or `refused`, the clean record is the player's own answer under the second claim, and if it is not on their menu (slot 4 taken by `m09_albion_notified`) he says it for them here: "My Recruiter offered you a name. You wouldn't buy it. I would have."; `""`, nothing. So an m09 player hears m09's Recruiter decision once at the core, never twice (§13 item 33 settled).
2. **The player's answers** (the talk-down): **three short exchanges**, one argument in three steps. Each time he makes a claim, the player picks an answer, and only an answer that meets that claim lands. He moves with the player: claims 2 and 3 each open on the exchange before (one opener after the rebuttal landed, one after a record answer landed, one after a miss), so he never concedes something that wasn't proved. **Four fixed slots per exchange** (m10 change 31): the mission's rebuttal; an evidence decoy about the same length, citing something found tonight against the wrong claim; and two record slots, where a plain decoy fills the first empty record slot. A record answer placed under the wrong claim takes a slot after slot 4. On a cold start the rebuttal is 2nd, 1st and 3rd of three buttons in the three exchanges, so no fixed button strategy reaches two points; the stage 1 play-through recorded a listener at 3 of 3 (m10 stage 1 §17). A landed point shows (D-20): he stops, looks at the console, a beat of silence, the music takes a step down, then a concession of exactly what was proved. A miss: he answers at once, dry, and nothing moves. Each pick writes `exchange_n` (`rebuttal` / `record` / `miss`) once; `points_landed` is derived from the three when step 5 opens, never counted as they land, so a resumed exchange can't count twice (m10 MC §6.3, P34). **Two of three for surrender.** Still easy, but the player has to listen. What the count buys is said before step 5: two or more, he stands with empty hands and offers cooperation; fewer, he stays seated and the arrest has a dangerous moment in it. Neither is a failure.
3. **The console.** **Stop it** (the disarm armed at the server hall) or **"Show me how it works"** (the first step towards Join, §4.3). Stopping it is one action and he doesn't fight it: "Then we do it the slow way. In court. I've a lot to say." On `m09_reading_finished`: "Then I was wrong about one thing.", or "Then I was wrong about two things." when the Reading's margin note guessed the player's bin wrong (`m09_cage_bin != "derek_file"`; m10 MC P32). Any open backdoor channel releases its queued batch now (the partial release; "Seattle first. As scheduled." on `m07_countdown_expired`), and the player hears it go, so a skipped backdoor task is felt here. `console_choice = stopped` and `partial_release` are written on "Stop it". If WREN is still free, she comes through the service door as the player reaches for the console to stop it (§4.4). "Show me how it works" is step 1 of Join; the next menu always has "[Step back.]" and "[Stop it.]".
4. **The database** (needs flag 4). Netherton comes on the line, and the two men speak for the first time in seven years: "Magnus." / "Adrian." (m10 change 28). Keep the map, give it to the people who can fix it, or burn every copy. Each option's case and cost are said **before** the menu opens, by the person with a reason to say them, and each has a cost to strangers, not only to the service (D-05, R2-12; m10 MC §6.5, P3, P30):
   - **The operations at stake.** Disclosure burns two live operations; securing keeps them, and the first rolls up a cell before the credits (through the cells ledger, §4.6). Netherton names the first two that apply, in this priority: (1) the watch on a recruited Victoria who held, was refused, or was trusted with Zero Day's share already dead (`m03_victoria_fate == "recruited"` and `victoria_gallery` in `confronted` / `refused`, or `trusted` with the share dead at the time, the same test `head_start_out` uses; milestone MS-02); (2) Nightshade's channel (`m08_nightshade_triple_agent and not m09_nightshade_shown_file`; a shown file spent it); (3) the HashChain tags (`m06_monitoring_enabled`); (4) the Critical Mass watch (`m09_completed and not m09_albion_notified`; sending the notice in m09 burned it), named by m04's branch ("the watch on Voltage's people", "what's left of his crew", or "the Critical Mass watch"); (5) the watch on the early-access order book, everyone who ordered the cells' head start, read off flag 2's schedule (every save); (6) the tap on Zero Day's brokers (unless a confronted Victoria burned it). There are always at least two; a cold start hears 5 and 6. Each rides a listed flaw, which is why disclosing burns it (Netherton: "Both of them run through holes on that list. Patch the holes and we lose the way in. I will sign it."). `vos_secret_used` does not burn the tap.
   - **What the player hears, in order.** Netherton makes secure's case, because it is his: "Half of these can't be patched this year. The water controllers can't be patched at all. Send those out and every buyer knows where to hit. Nobody can stop them." HaX gives secure's cost: "Infusion pumps in a hundred and forty hospitals. The one he used on you. Still live next year if we do this." Netherton gives disclose's cost and reason (above). HaX: "The ones nobody can patch go to the operators, with workarounds. Not into a public list."; on `m09_albion_notified`, "You burned the Critical Mass watch for one letter. This is that letter, a thousand times." HaX gives wipe's cost, plainly, with the menu open: "If both copies die, this room purges itself. Doors, lights, everything. You won't have him after." Netherton: "Then nobody has it. Not him, not us. Nobody fixes anything either. We both answer for it." Asked to choose ("[HaX. What would you do?]"), HaX refuses: "No. This one's yours." Netherton states no preference.
   - **Secure** the stolen copy as evidence; SAFETYNET keeps its master. Gain: the flaws nobody can fix yet stay out of buyers' hands; both named operations keep running, and the first rolls up a cell. Cost: the hoard stands; the card says "The database: back in the vault. Infusion pumps in 140 hospitals are still unpatched."; he was right about the service, and the player's hand proved it. (`secured`)
   - **Disclose** (the button says "coordinated disclosure", and so does the line before the menu; D-02): wipe Tomb Gamma's copy and send SAFETYNET's master to the vendors on a coordinated-disclosure schedule, at its true speed: acknowledgements tonight, fixes in about ninety days, a year before most are fitted (the same timescale m09's notice uses). Cost: both named operations go dark at 06:00, and one public cost: operators told of flaws with no fix run by hand ("Three water utilities went to manual at five. There's no patch for their controllers. They'll turn the valves by hand till spring."). He signs anyway (user-approved, F6). A player who sent the Albion notice in m09 has done this once already. (`disclosed`)
   - **Wipe both**. SAFETYNET left Nightshade's door into its vault open on purpose, as a trace to follow the theft here (D-13; canon, F7), and that line is still live. Through it the player can erase SAFETYNET's master too. Gain: nobody holds the map, not him, not the cells' customers, not the service; it is the one outcome his argument can't win, shown at 06:00 ("Nobody holds the map now. Not him, not the buyers, not whoever sits in my chair next."). Cost: nothing is fixed, every flaw is still out there, unmapped; the core purges itself and he goes in the dark; the end-for-him choice is gone; the player is suspended. This is Scorched Earth (§4.5). (`both_wiped`)
5. **Tesseract** (not after "wipe both"). User decisions F11 (two lethal-force versions, each signposted; m10 change 20) and F13 (surrender is a deal with no public trial; arrest is a public trial with no cells; m10 MC P4). On `join_step >= 2` (the player asked for the key, then stepped away) he opens with one line whatever the points: "You held your hand out for it. I won't forget that." (P19.)
   - **Two or more points:** he stands with his hands empty. "Ask me, then." Netherton, on the line, says what each option means before the menu: "If he cooperates, there is no trial. He gives us cells, and nobody hears his speech. Arrest him, and he makes it in open court." The player accepts his cooperation ("[Then cooperate. All of it.]", **surrender**: he types the cells' contact protocol into the bunker's terminal himself, two cells go into the ledger, no public trial and his argument never tested in court; he warns the player, "They'll work out who you are by Thursday. Be careful."), arrests him anyway ("[No deal. You're under arrest.]", **arrest**: a public trial, HaX testifies, no protocol and no cells, and he gets the platform he wanted), or takes the cold choice below. Justified lethal force is not offered here (D-11, reworded: it covers only the justified version).
   - **Fewer than two:** "Arrest me, then." The menu: arrest him, or the cold choice. On "arrest", as the player moves, **he makes a move that threatens lives**: his hand goes to the console, where one key sends the first city's list out anyway (an act he makes, not a dead-man switch). A two-option beat, no timer: **[Fire.]** (**justified lethal force**, `lethal`, one narrated line, certain; HaX: "I was a second behind you. Log it. I'll sign under you."), or **[Hold fire.]**, and HaX kills the feed through the host the player now controls, in time ("Got it. Feed's dead. Hold. He's done."); he says "Ninety-nine." and holds out his wrists (**arrest**). The player chooses on the one fact HaX gave them at flag 4 ("I can try to kill the feed. Try."); holding fire always works, and the player doesn't know that.
   - **The cold choice**, offered in both menus and **always the last option** (P18): "[Shoot him where he sits.]" on fewer than two points, "[Shoot him where he stands.]" on two or more. The narrator's warning: "His hands are empty and in plain sight. He isn't moving. If you fire now, it's an execution, and HaX is on the line." Then "[Do it.]" or "[Lower the weapon.]". Only "Do it." sets `ending = executed`. "Lower the weapon." returns to the same menu **without** the cold option for the rest of the scene ("You lower it. He hasn't moved."; `cold_lowered`; orchestrator ruling). It can't be reached by a misclick, a knock-out, the clock, a failed talk-down or any import. No other button in the scene uses "shoot"; the justified button's "[Fire.]" never shares words with the cold labels.

**The three exchanges.** Each has the mission's own rebuttal, always on the menu, so surrender is open to any player. Under it, at most two answers from the player's record. Each record slot has its own pool of **at most two candidates** (milestone ruling, D-05's simplification list), most specific first, so on an m09 save the generic m09 answers can't crowd out the player's own choices (m10 MC §6.3, P6; this supersedes m09's "ranked last" for the clean record, P22). Lines in m10 stage 2 §2.3 and m10 MC §6.3:

| His claim | The mission's rebuttal | From the record (record slot 3 pool; record slot 4 pool; most specific first) |
|---|---|---|
| "The cost is acceptable." | Someone corrected his casualty sheet and he signed the old number: the bench copy on the workshop floor's critical path carries Asante's correction (the model credits the hospitals' recovery twice, so it understates the dead) and his second signature under the unchanged figure (m10 change 29). Decoding the margin strip (optional; Base64, one step, in the start kit's CyberChef) names Asante, gives her corrected range, 2,100 to 3,600, and the password to her model on a bench terminal (`model_terminal`, password `DOUBLECOUNT`); reading the model sets `margin_note_read`, which sharpens the rebuttal (m10 S6-02). Evidence decoy: his schedule puts the hospitals first (order, not price). Plain decoy: he'll be in a cell before the month is out (about him). | Slot 3: `m07_debrief_stance = hardened` (the live curve on the projection desk) > the clean first day (with `m01_completed`, no m01 KOs, no `m02_patient_assaulted`, and `ko_pattern() == 0`, §3.6). Slot 4: `m02_bed4_manually_stabilised` > Derek's file (`m09_cage_bin = derek_file`: "[You priced Derek Lawson before anyone hired him. You don't pay your costs. You hand them out.]" / "*pause* Derek paid it. I didn't.") |
| "Services like yours hoard. Only a shock fixes it." He gives it a victim: the infusion-pump flaw in 140 hospitals, known for three years and called an asset. | "Only a shock" is untested, because he never tried a letter: nine years ago he argued the Albion flaw should go to the vendor, lost, and never sent the notice he drafted (from the m09 papers, or HaX's flag 4 lines on a cold start). Evidence decoy: the pump flaw is on his own schedule (the price again). | Slot 3, earlier missions: `m04_disclosure_choice = full` ("And nobody died for it") > `m02_exposed_hospital`. Slot 4, Helena: `m09_albion_notified` ("I sent your notice. Tonight, from Helena.") > on `m09_recruiter_offer` `named` or `refused`, the clean record in m09's wording, "[Your Recruiter offered me a name. I wouldn't buy it from her.]", answered by value (`named`: "*pause* No. You'd rather the slow way. It worked."; `refused`: "*pause* No. You let her keep her people sooner than pay her. I didn't think your service still did that."); if it is hidden, he says it in the Recruiter beat (step 1) |
| "Someone has to make it hurt. Better me, with clean hands." He starts the line ("Let it hurt afterwards, not during.") and ends "I'd rather it was me". | The clean-hands argument (m10 change 31): "Better you? You need my hand on that key so yours stays clean." His concession gives up exactly that ("Better me, I said. And I brought you."). Both decoys answer earlier claims ("target list"; "Nobody elected you"). | Slot 2 (the first record slot in this exchange), HaX: the speaker answer, "[HaX. You're on speaker.]" (`m09_hax_confession_answer` is `same_in_your_place` or `afterwards`, with HaX's "All right. Let him hear it from me." first on `afterwards`; m10 change 38), or on `should_have_told` "[She learned that from you.]", opened by HaX's unasked line just before the menu ("My agent told me I sat on it. They were right. You never told me anything."). Slot 4 (capped at two): his Tomb Alpha teaching notes (`m09_deck_pulled = alpha_tapes`; "You told them to carry it. You never told them to hand it on.") > what HaX does with the line, told in m09 (`m09_completed`): you keep your own hurt for afterwards so you can do the job, and you never hand it to strangers (row 120). Cut by the cap: `m09_reading_finished` ("Your file says I press it."; its "wrong about one thing" payoff stays), Voltage's hands or his stand-down, and a technician talked round; the last two keep their "In this operation" clauses. On `m07_architect_echo_heard`, HaX has one beat as he says the line. Whatever the player picks here, the knock-out pattern's ledger sting comes first when it applies (§3.6) |

The decoys are fixed lines per exchange (an evidence decoy and a plain decoy, the plain decoy filling the first empty record slot), plus record answers placed under the wrong claim, after slot 4. The rebuttal's cold-start position (2nd, 1st, 3rd) is unchanged by the pools. He answers some of the player's lines from his ledger, which sting first and decide nothing: `m04_crew_lost`, `m02_patient_bed4_deceased`, `m06_satoshi_stance`, and the knock-out pattern in exchange 3 (§3.6, F16).

### 4.2 The Publication's channels and partial release

The Publication goes out through up to two channels, and the cells get their head start through a third. (Social Fabric's story machine was a fourth; with the media room cut, its story is a prop and runs from outside on uncovered Fracture, row 84.) Only the first must be shut to stop the Publication; the others decide the cost.

| Channel | Present when | Shut by | If left open |
|---|---|---|---|
| Tomb Gamma's own release servers | always | the core (step 3) | the key is pressed (Join): everything, everywhere, in the same minute. The 06:00 fallback (city by city) stays on the countdown as menace and never fires before the core in normal play |
| The cells' head start | always | the server-hall console (needs flag 2), a dilemma on every save: kill Zero Day's share, kill Ghost's buyers' (bait branch only, where Ghost's share is live), or let Zero Day's run and trace it ("I can kill one feed, or follow one. Not both. Pick."; m10 MC C9, P29); or a slot-holder stops their share in the scene. Zero Day's share: recruited Victoria confronted, or trusted once the share is dead; escaped Victoria talked down (with the threat) or let walk on her trade (without it); or arrested Victoria's information used on Vos (P1). Ghost's share: deal kept, parley, or Ghost talked out of the sale | always set for 04:00; flag 2 is where the player finds it (HaX: "It's set for four. It goes when the outer lock opens."); every live share goes out at **one moment, the outer lock's unlock event**, whichever way it was opened (the lock's countdown object reads 04:00, then "EARLY ACCESS: RELEASED", or "NOTHING TO RELEASE" when every share was stopped; Tesseract says which as his first line in the core, and HaX on her return, §4.1 step 0), so every gallery decision about Zero Day's share is in time (m10 change 19). HaX warns twice: after flag 2, and as the player leaves the server hall ("Fifteen minutes to their head start. That console's your last chance at it."). Skipped, every live share goes untraced. The trace gets Netherton's 06:00 line, "We followed Zero Day's share. Four buyers, and one of them was careless.", and a cell in the ledger; a share stopped in the gallery leaves nothing to trace ("She cancelled it. Nothing to follow.") |
| The Trojan Horse backdoor | `m07_completed and (m07_team_assignment != "trojan_horse" or m07_team_redirected)` (the redirect also sets `trojan_horse`; never played m07 means no channel) | an optional console task in the server hall (needs flag 2) | **partial release**: the batch already queued on the backdoor goes out when the core is shut. Small on `m07_team_redirected` (14 million systems), large when Austin was never covered (47 million) |
**The clock** (R3-02; m10 change 25). Two marks, **04:00 UTC** (the cells' head start) and **06:00 UTC** (the fallback), driven by story events, not wall-clock time, so slow VM work never costs the player anything. There is no countdown engine feature and none is needed: time is shown by lines and by a countdown object in each of the eight main-path rooms whose text reads one string global, `raid_clock`, set by mappings on story beats (orchestrator ruling; object text variants already read globals, confirmed by the F14 trial). Stage 4 fixed the values (m10 OT §6): `t0145` the briefing, `t0200` the split, `t0210` Arakawa's call, `t0220` the pair back, `t0230` the breach, `t0245` the security office, `t0300` the workshop, `t0315` the power room, `t0330` the server hall, `t0345` leaving it (the gallery stair opening), `t0400` the outer lock, `t0600` when HaX's rest in the 06:00 scene turns the forward post's screen over (a HaX mapping on `global_variable_changed:six_done`, with the forward-only guard), **not** as the scene opens: the scene opens "a few minutes before six" and the clock reads `t0400` until then (ruling 16, from m10-R M-3; the dev log's ruling at `scenarios/FINALE_DEV_LOG.md` ~L358). `six_done` is set on the scene's last line, so the `t0600` screen text is never seen in play (the narrator says it); on `scorched` it is set before the hearing, which is correct. Each value carries a `t` because ink's `#set_global` turns digits into numbers; every step only moves the clock forward, so a reload's walk back never winds it back. The objects are the authority for every spoken time, and stage 7 checks each line against them (m10 S4-10). People talk about it. The lock's object reads 04:00 as the lock opens, which is when every live share goes ("before four" and "as the lock opens" are the same beat); HaX warns the player twice (§4.2 table). Stepping out between flags 2 and 3 costs nothing. It never reaches 06:00 before the core; at the core the object holds the last beat's time while they talk (he doesn't pause it, m10 change 37), and on Join every object jumps to RELEASED. At 06:00, in the forward-post cutscene, the object on the table reaches 06:00 and nothing happens. The first city's batch ("Seattle first. As scheduled.") goes only through an open backdoor channel, as the partial release when the core is shut. Nothing on the clock ends the mission or forces an ending. With Austin covered directly and every live share of the head start stopped (one at the console, the other in its scene), nothing is released at all.

**The head start's state is visible** (milestone D-03). From flag 2 on, every countdown object carries two status lines under the time, set by the same globals the design already computes: "ZERO DAY: LIVE / STOPPED / TRACED" and "SECOND BUYER: LIVE / STOPPED / NONE" (Ghost's share; NONE where Ghost never sold). HaX's hub gains a topic, "[What's still live?]", which reads the two lines aloud with the next place each can be stopped. This only displays state; no rule changes. Stage 4's values (m10 OT §6): ZERO DAY reads LIVE / STOPPED / TRACED / RELEASED (a traced share that went at the lock reads OUT, TRACED, m10 S4-17), SECOND BUYER reads LIVE / STOPPED / NONE / RELEASED, and "Ghost" never appears on a screen. The object text reads globals as the engine stands (F14 trial: no change needed); an ERB helper writes the combinations, since conditions take `&&` only.

**What the player sees of the database.** The server hall's schedule (flag 2) lists the first entries by city in plain words: a hospital group's infusion pumps, a county's dispatch software, a water utility's controllers (D-04). Closing the backdoor and killing the story are the hands-on version of "who gets hurt first", and the console's kill-or-trace is the database question in small (stop the harm, or let it run and learn who takes it), so the core's menu is the player's conclusion, not the lesson.

### 4.3 Join ENTROPY: signposting

The one non-success ending. A deliberate choice the player makes three times, never a trap:

Every player hears the offer as part of his case (step 1), so listening to the villain is never a step towards betrayal (D-21). Join starts only with an action:

1. At step 3 the player chooses **"Show me how it works."** He shows them the key: "It's a key. You press it. Everything I've told you happens." His next line goes by the points (P19, M-11): on 0–1 landed exchanges, "You couldn't answer me. Then let it be you."; on 2–3, colder, "You've beaten me. Now you want the key?" The narrator says plainly what pressing it means, with **the casualty figure, said once, here** (P40): "Pressing it publishes the database to everyone, everywhere, in the same minute. Eleven hundred to twenty-six hundred people, the first month, by his own figure. You can still step back." The next menu always includes "[Step back.]" and "[Stop it.]".
2. The player chooses **"Give me the key."** HaX, on the line: "0x00. Don't." Narrator: "If you take it, the mission ends here, and you work for ENTROPY." "[Step away.]" and "[Stop it.]" are offered again.
3. The release key is at the console, labelled, with its own confirmation ("Publish the database to everyone, now?"), built as an ink choice inside his conversation, since the engine's confirmation action can't return into an open conversation (m10 S5-3, RL §6 item 45); "[No.]" returns to step 2's menu. Only that press sets the ending (`ending = joined`, `console_choice = joined`, `database_outcome = published`), and it makes the Reading's prediction true. The key sends everything everywhere in the same minute; he says, quietly, "Thank you, Agent." and nothing else. WREN never comes through on this path.

**Stepping back is remembered** (`join_step`, 0–3, the furthest step reached; m10 MC §6.7, P19). Step 1 is curiosity and costs nothing. Asking for the key (`join_step >= 2`) is paid once, by his line opening step 5 ("You held your hand out for it. I won't forget that."), and nothing else; HaX heard it and says nothing.

It cannot be reached by a misclick, a knock-out, the clock, a failed talk-down, or any import. `m01_player_launched_attack` (he makes the offer to that player first and most directly) and `m06_satoshi_stance` (`wondered` warmer; `rejected` and `refused` colder, each with its own line, row 76) change how Tesseract makes the offer, and the points change his step 1 line; nothing changes the signposting. m01's LAUNCH button is the precedent.

### 4.4 What earlier choices change

The route through the bunker works with every import at its default. These change the cost and who is standing at the end.

In the gallery her information is used automatically: Vos withdraws the order and stands aside (D-04: no use-or-keep) | KO'd or never played, or her call abandoned: Vos in the gallery, cold, with his trade (the code card for his name off the report) | buy or refuse (D-04: the talk-out is cut); the long way |

Watch my hands."; he asks one thing for the inquiry) or does it by hand while he talks them through it (the gallery camera switch, which takes the gallery's monitor guard off his post with a bark). Otherwise the default technician Wendell Briggs at the panel, with Vance, else Elena, else HaX in the phone slot: tell Wendell what the servers are for (he cuts the shutter and rings Dale on the next shift, who stays home; but the call goes through the switchboard, so the server hall is warned: Asante won't talk, Mercer can't be drawn off) or work round him (the hand cut and the camera switch; Dale walks into the raid at shift change; m10 MC P36). Both mappings carry `voiced: true` and a `targetKnot`; when the voice is HaX the re-ring restarts at its knot rather than using `resume`, and the shared knot ends `#exit_conversation`, so the hub topic doesn't fall back into her hub (milestone S4–5 F-03, A8). On the default branch Wendell's cost ("He doesn't know. Tell him…") is said before the pick, by the slot voice on entering the power room or by HaX (m10 MC rule 1.2, milestone S4–5 A11) |
| Workshop | Gary protected: at the door as the player opens it ("You saved my career."); he recognises the bench's casing and the default technician Imran Siddiqui, whom he trained, and the player talks Imran round (he goes home with his phone, names the bench log and sends Farah Qureshi home from the gallery shift) or takes him in (the supplier's delivery read off his phone, the supplier rolled up; Farah stays on shift) | Gary unprotected: on the floor beside Imran, who follows Gary: Gary talked round (a witness), left in place with his card cloned (still on the payroll, shipping nothing) or cuffed | talk, clone the technician's card, or go back to the security office for the fitter's spare card (a pickable locker; a boss-key return trip that spends the pick, m10 S5-10) |
| Server hall | Asante, who can be talked into showing the schedule and, asked, confirms her correction to his sheet (sharpening the first rebuttal without the decode) | Mercer at large (with `m07_completed`): a hostile boss with one line. | Guards | `m06_assets_seized`: two posts empty, gap in the fence, named by HaX on arrival | full staffing | stealth and the cloner as in any mission |

| The gallery's guards | each effect carried in gets a bark, since HaX is jammed there (P38): "Camera's dead again. I'll go and look." (the switch); "Where's Farah? She was on till six." (Imran); "Who gave out the code?" (Ghost's code at the keypad, on `ghost_gift === 'code'`; the old "Who changed the code?" is retired, m10 CONTRACT 10-11, milestone A27); Nightshade on the lock's intercom, "Go on. I've got the door." Exactly three choices in the mission pay in a guard (the tunnel post, the monitor post, Farah's post), each voiced | – | – |

**The whodunit** (row 117; R2-04, R2-06, R2-03; m10 stage 1 §8; m10 MC §5). A puzzle with a moral cost, optional, any time after clue 1. Its aim ("Find the sleeper") opens at the private aside, story-gated rather than on an import, and its tasks are the clues, never the name (m10 S4-19, OT §1–2). Skipped whenever m09 named WREN. On `taken`, `sleeper_caught` is true from the start: Kowalczyk is cuffed at the briefing, nobody props the service door, the door log has no 02:10 entry, the bench shows the cloner shipped "to WREN" and never used ("still in the case"), and the core has no WREN beat. On `double_crossed` she was warned and is gone: no whodunit, no sleeper on the team, and she waits at the core (below). Otherwise three clues, spread through the bunker. No clue names WREN on its own; clues 2 and 3 cross at one name. HaX's nudges say only what she can see and never name a suspect.

**What WREN did, and for whom** (m09 change 8, m10 change 27). The Initiative placed her eleven years ago; Tesseract took her tasking for tonight: see 0x00 safely to the core, and keep the core's service door open as his exit. She believes in the Publication, and her one goal at the core is the key. The suspects each have a visible reason, a beat, and something that clears them (m10 change 18): Quintero (argued against Helena and tonight; took the drain alone; the other Harwick & Lowe hire, father in care; cleared by clue 3), Kowalczyk (always a room ahead; took the door end; packed the case; cleared by nothing), Malhotra (his badge on the log; eleven years' service, the same as WREN's placement, m10 change 4; cleared by clue 2), Arakawa (a lateral hire who closed the county road, as he cleared Helena's police; packed the case; cleared by clue 2), and Vector and Phantom where present (cleared by clue 2).

1. **Door log** (security office, needs flag 1): at 02:10 the core's service door opened to a SAFETYNET badge, Malhotra's. HaX: "The log says Malhotra's badge. Malhotra was on with me at ten past." So somebody has a copy. Asked for the service door's camera: "That camera's a box on a bracket. Never wired." (m10 change 36.)
2. **Alibi** (on the line, any time after clue 1): who wasn't where somebody could see them at 02:10? The player saw Arakawa at the gate, on his call; HaX had Malhotra; Netherton had Vector and Phantom. That leaves the tunnel pair, Quintero and Kowalczyk, and since both branches of the fork come out by the service door, either could have opened it.
3. **The bench log** (workshop floor; m10 change 22): beside the supplier batches (the crackers Nightshade put into stores) is a cloner shipped "to WREN" in Task Force Null's breaching case, and the record it sent home: Malhotra's badge, copied at 01:58. At 01:58 the case was open on the kit-check table; its sign-out sheet on the forward-post table, readable any time and read out by HaX from her hub if asked ("[Who signed for the case?]", m10 S4-14), says "Opened: 01:52. Kowalczyk / Arakawa." (on `taken`, "Opened: 01:52. Arakawa." with both packers' initials, H.K. and K.A., so the unused cloner points at nobody new; on `double_crossed`, Arakawa alone; m10 S6 §1.1, §5). Quintero was at the map with Netherton throughout, so the packers were the only hands in the case. The cracker is not a clue: the bench logs it by batch with no recipient (row 29).

**Helpers who replace a clue** (m10 change 11): Nightshade, kept or dealt, replaces clue 2, with ENTROPY's usual method, since he speaks before the split ("They always borrow a badge, and it's never the borrower's. Look for whoever went where nobody watches."); `m09_sleeper_shortlist` replaces clue 2 (HaX at the private aside, on `not m09_sleeper_named`); Phantom's list replaces clue 3 (the case went out with one more unit than its sheet lists, signed for by Kowalczyk and Arakawa). Imran, talked round, points at the bench log by name without replacing it. None is the answer on its own. A warned WREN (the tripwire) goes ahead a room early, at the workshop, so the face-to-face window is shorter (m10 change 9).

**Accusing**, any time after clue 1: face to face when the accused is in the room, **on the line** to HaX (passed to Netherton) when not, with the same outcomes (m10 change 3). It is reached through the HaX hub ("[I know who WREN is.]"), then the team members on tonight's raid, then one confirmation ("[Yes. Them.]" / "[Not yet.]"). The cost of not accusing is said at the aside (Netherton's "Find them before you get there.") and by HaX after the third clue ("That's three things that point somewhere. Your call, and you don't have to make it."). Kowalczyk's last face-to-face moment is her peel-off in the server hall, or the security office on the tether branch. **One wrong accusation at a time** (m10 MC P35): while an officer is out, HaX refuses a second accusation of anyone else until the first is recalled ("Get Malhotra back first."); the right one is always accepted.
- **Right, face to face:** four cards in order, and she answers the first two with lies: the alibi ("Malhotra's badge, Malhotra's door. Ask him."), the cloner in the case ("I packed that case with Kenji. Ask him where he was at ten past."), Arakawa at the gate (broken from the player's memory), and her own words ("You said it never went through stores. Then it came straight off his bench."; on an m09 save, HaX's "The long way is over" comes back here). She cracks and is held at the forward post. `sleeper_caught` is true. She asks one question, "When did you know?" (m10 MC C18, a stance her 06:00 reply pays: "The paperwork. It's always the paperwork." / "No, you didn't. But thank you." / "That's worse. That means I was good."), then "I liked you. I'd still have pressed it." The player is offered only answers they could give (stage 7 R2 A2-04): the bench answer "[At the bench log. Then your name on the sign-out sheet.]" needs `clue_bench_log`; "[At the briefing. You laughed too easily.]" and "[I didn't. I worked it out.]" are always there; at 06:00, a Kowalczyk nobody named (she came through his door) gets one answer only, "[I didn't. Not till you came through his door.]".
- **Right, on the line:** Netherton sends Quintero by the service side to take her at the door; Kowalczyk asks her question at 06:00 instead. With no Quintero to send, WREN reaches the core and the player stops her there; `sleeper_caught` is still true.
- **Wrong:** the innocent officer is led back with one line that is also a hint (Malhotra: "Eleven years. Check the log again."). The team loses that officer's job (m10 MC §5, P50): without Malhotra, HaX's line drops one room early (the wiring, and whether this cost stands or changes, is m10 OT's, one rule in arc, MC and OT; milestone S4–5 A5); without Arakawa, the county road (the sheriff reopens it without his liaison, and Netherton has a deputy at the gate asking who for; on the tether branch the van team holds the blast door instead); without Quintero, nobody but the player stops WREN at the core; without Vector or Phantom, the shared cost: their job at the forward post goes, with one line from Netherton ("Then I am a pair of hands short. Carry on."), and no room effect of its own. `wrong_accusation` is true, and stays true after a recall. Once the player holds the clearing evidence, Netherton **recalls** the officer over HaX's channel ("Officer Malhotra, you are cleared. Come back, and leave the face at the van."); their job returns from the next room, and the credits say "Recalled.". Never a death, never a blocked route.
- **None:** she acts at the core.

Knocking out a raid member met in the bunker holds them at the forward post but is **not** an accusation (Malhotra, at the van end all night, is `koImmune`, m10 RL §2.1); it costs an epilogue line, and each knockable raid member has a KO global (`<name>_ko`, via `globalVarOnKO`); there is no `malhotra_ko`, since a `koImmune` record can never set one (milestone S4–5 F-09). A held raid member can still be accused on the line, with the same outcomes. A knocked-out Kowalczyk can't act at the core; if the player never names her, `sleeper_caught` stays false and the epilogue notes she was never named.

**WREN at the core** (m10 MC §6.4). If she is free (not caught, not knocked out, not on the double-cross branch, not on the Join path), the service door she propped opens as the player reaches for the console to stop it, and she comes through for the key. Tesseract refuses her, every time: "Not you, Halina. When they find out what you are, it proves nothing. It has to be one of theirs." The player hears his motive from his own mouth. She says one word, "Adrian.", and tries anyway. Then, by branch:
- **Default:** Quintero comes in from the lock at the noise and is wounded stopping her (a shoulder; she's talking at 06:00; `quintero_wounded`).
- **On `m09_deck_pulled = alpha_tapes`** (m09 MC §9 item 25, m10 MC P32): HaX knows his habits from the tapes and warns as the player reaches the console: "Service door. He always builds his own way out first." The beat runs the same, refusal included, but Quintero, warned and already moving, takes her **after** his refusal and before she touches the key, **unhurt**.
- **With no Quintero** (held, knocked out, sent on a line accusation, or wrongly accused and not recalled): the player stops her with a one-option "[Stop her.]" (an ink beat: the engine has no knock-out tag, so the choice sets `wren_stopped` and her core copy hides itself on its own mapping; m10 S5-5, RL §6 item 40); on `alpha_tapes` the warning goes to the player and the beat comes a second earlier.

The disarm goes through in every case. Stopping her at the core is **not an accusation**: `sleeper_caught` stays as it was (false, unless she had been named on the line), and `wren_at_core` is true. WREN caught: he glances at the service door, nobody comes, "You found her. I wondered whether you would."; named on the line with no Quintero to send: "You found her. Too late to stop her walking in." On the Join path she never comes through: he already has the hand he wanted. If the player steps back from Join and then stops it, she comes as normal (§13 item 29).

**The double-cross branch** (`m09_recruiter_offer = double_crossed`; m09 MC ch. 5, m10 MC P31; orchestrator ruling). Kowalczyk is in the core when the player walks in, a known face standing by the service door, unarmed, the cracker's twin on her belt. The refusal has already happened; the player arrives on the end of it ("...it proves nothing. It has to be one of theirs." / "Adrian."). HaX: "Quintero. Core. Now." Quintero comes in from the lock and takes her, unhurt (with no Quintero, the player takes her with a one-option beat); Kowalczyk doesn't fight and says one thing on her way out: "She told me you'd break it. The Recruiter. She was right about you." No choice: the double-cross was the choice, made in Helena. `sleeper_caught` and `wren_at_core` are true, `quintero_wounded` false. **Nobody is wounded at the core on this branch**: a known face waiting in plain view can't come through a door at the key. That clean core is the double-cross's one gain in m10, against what it cost in Helena (the name's use, half her network, the player's word). Then step 0 runs as normal.

### 4.5 The endings

| Ending | Reached by | `ending` | Credits title |
|---|---|---|---|
| Arrest (a public trial, no cells; F13) | stop; secure or disclose; then either two points and "[No deal. You're under arrest.]", or fewer than two, "arrest", and "[Hold fire.]" when his hand goes to the console | `arrest` | MISSION COMPLETE |
| Surrender and cooperation (a deal, no public trial; F13) | stop; secure or disclose; two or more points; "[Then cooperate. All of it.]" | `surrender` | MISSION COMPLETE |
| Lethal force, justified | stop; secure or disclose; fewer than two points; "arrest"; [Fire.] when his hand goes to the console | `lethal` | MISSION COMPLETE |
| Lethal force, cold | stop; secure or disclose; either point count; the labelled cold choice, then "[Do it.]" (§4.1 step 5) | `executed` | MISSION COMPLETE, then AGENT 0x00: IN CUSTODY |
| Scorched Earth | stop; wipe both | `scorched` | MISSION COMPLETE, then AGENT 0x00: SUSPENDED |
| Join ENTROPY | the three steps of §4.3 | `joined` | MISSION FAILED, with the Publication's casualty projection (as m01's launch ending): his figure, 1,100 to 2,600, and "CORRECTED: 2,100 TO 3,600.", always printed (m10 S6 §3.6) |

**Lethal force, cold** (F11; m10 changes 20, 40). The darkest ending; its weight is the people who have to answer for it. One narrated line: his hands are empty, and the player fires. HaX says nothing for a long time, then the player's designation, once. No walkabout: Quintero comes in from the lock and takes the player's weapon without a word (if she is wounded or was sent back, Netherton's van team does it at the lock). At the forward post Netherton signs nothing: "I will not put my name under this one. You will be held until the board sits." HaX doesn't come to the van, and does not tell the player "the rest"; her last word is a later message: "I'll be at the board. I don't know what I'll say." At 06:00 Social Fabric sends a new version of its story with the player's photo on it, from outside, on every branch (running: updated; held: the draft goes out; the media room is cut, row 84). There is no "massacre"; its one new paragraph, that a SAFETYNET agent shot an unarmed man in his own bunker, is true. It appears on the forward post's screen, headline, photo and the true paragraph, with **no narrator line**: the player reads it themselves (m10 MC §8.3, P39; the earlier narrator line is withdrawn). The shot itself is one line: "He is still looking at you when you fire." `media_story_ran` is true. The card's first line is "Dr Adrian Tesseract: shot unarmed."; the rest of the night's work still lands (the database's beat, the cells, the people in "In this operation"), because the ending punishes the act, not the whole night. The $12.8M line doesn't play: Netherton signs nothing.

**Scorched Earth.** The chaos is his own dead-man purge (D-12), triggered by the wipe. He wired the core to purge itself if both copies died, because that is the one outcome where he can't win the argument and can't be made to answer for it. The core's fire suppression dumps, the lights go, the blast doors cycle, and in the dark he is gone by the service door WREN propped (or his own way, if she was caught). The player gets out with the team. Nobody holds the map, SAFETYNET included, and nobody fixes anything. At 06:00 Netherton suspends the player in front of the team: "I wanted to do the same. That is why you are suspended and I am not." He also gives the wipe's gain, crediting only the wipe: "Nobody holds the map now. Not him, not the buyers, not whoever sits in my chair next." (m10 MC §6.5). A short hearing scene follows, with HaX beside the player; it opens off the 06:00 scene's last line, and its own last line closes the night (§4.6, m10 OT §4.7).

### 4.6 Epilogue beats

Every ending except Join runs the same frame (m10 changes 5, 14, 33): the ending's own beat at the core, then **the forward post at 06:00**, as a cutscene under floodlights (nobody walks back up the corridor), then the close. Join's frame is the Citadel. **The close, in order:** the conclusion screen (`bond_visualiser`, titled by ending), the closing card, the teaser slot (§7), the credits roll. **Budget: about 8 minutes** from the ending's own beat to the end of the roll (milestone D-05), inside an end-to-end m10 of about 105; stage 4 set the per-beat figures: the ending's own beat 1, 06:00 5, card and teaser 1, the credits' opening 1 (m10 OT §8).

**How the close is wired** (m10 OT §4.7, §7; S4-5; m08's order). The ending's own beat plays inside his conversation after the pick. The closing scene (`six_oclock`, `citadel`, and `hearing` after `six_oclock` on Scorched Earth) is a hidden person NPC in the start room, opened by its own mapping when **his conversation closes** with `ending` set, so nothing opens over the ending's beat; every closing mapping carries `!closing_done` and `onceOnly`, with a `game_loaded` backstop per scene. The last scene's last line completes `close_the_night` (the conclusion aim's task) and sets `closing_done`; the music event on that scene's `conversation_closed` plays the ending's track and carries the card and credits. There is one credits entry per closing NPC and ending value, never a second one, so a replayed scene can't roll the credits twice. `concludeRequires` is the four flag tasks only (§4).

**The closing sequence's engine home** (milestone D-09; F14, committed in 859da19d; `docs/agents/FINALE_ENGINE_TRIAL_CALLS_CREDITS.md`). A music event entry carries `closingCard` next to `credits`: `closingCard.lines` are `{ text, condition }`, shown in order, the first five that hold, as one held block after the conclusion screen; `closingCard.teaser` runs only while its `enabledBy` global is true (default `teaser_enabled`). `credits` takes `section` blocks: `people`, each a name with `clauses` of which the first that holds prints (one clause per person without hand-made exclusions), and `lines`; a heading prints only if something under it does. Conditions are JavaScript and name `globalVars.`; every string is fixed. This replaces the `{ when, text, block, order }` format sketched at the milestone; "In this operation" and "Elsewhere" are two `section` blocks. Stage 7 writes the card and clauses in this format. The validator doesn't check these keys yet, so stage 9 reads them by hand (engine limit).

**At 06:00.** First a short walkabout (every ending that reaches 06:00 except the cold one): the player picks whom to go to among the people the night left standing there, one line each, **at most three picks** (milestone ruling, D-05), Quintero always first on the list (on a stretcher on `quintero_wounded`, answering the briefing stance once more). The rest are offered in this precedence, and the player picks up to two more: Kowalczyk in the van if caught (her reply to "When did you know?"); a wrongly accused officer not recalled; then Voltage, Gary and Imran, Wendell, Victoria, by branch (m10 MC §8.2). Whoever isn't picked keeps their credit clause. **Fixed beats outside the walkabout**, so the cap of three can never drop them (orchestrator ruling at the m10 milestone fixes; m10 MC §8 table): a shown Nightshade left at the post speaks as the cutscene opens ("You left me where they couldn't see me. I'm still useful. That's rarer than you'd think."); HaX's line on the forty-seven on `m09_recruiter_secret` `kept` or `denied` (harder on `denied`) comes before "the rest"; and on `m09_recruiter_offer = named`, Netherton's "The arrest teams are at the doors. They go on the hour." (D-01). Then Netherton and HaX in a fixed order: Netherton's lines with numbers, tonight's cost only (the head start, `head_start_out`; on the trace, "We followed Zero Day's share. Four buyers, and one of them was careless."; the media story, a knocked-out raid member, the Zero Day tap and the $12.8M on `m06_assets_seized`, row 80, behind an optional prompt), the database's beat, the van on arrest and surrender, then HaX's promise from Helena, **"the rest"**, two lines: "The night he left, he asked me to go with him." / "I said no. It took me till morning." (m09 change 15, m10 changes 21, 39). Her last word comes as the table's countdown object reaches 06:00 and nothing happens.

**06:00 as shipped** (`m10_six_oclock.ink`, after m10 script edit R3; the contract and the ink win where this file differs):
- **Opening.** "The forward post, a few minutes before six." (narrator), so the screen can turn over to six on HaX's line. Fixed beats: the shown, not-taken-in Nightshade's "stay" line; Netherton's `named` line above.
- **Walkabout.** Three picks, then straight on; "[That's everyone.]" leaves early. Quintero first (lines by `quintero_ko`, a wrong accusation of her, her stance, `quintero_wounded`, else "Everybody walked out. That's all I wanted from tonight."). Then Kowalczyk at the van window when `sleeper_caught or wren_at_core` (on `double_crossed`, "Eleven years. You'd think I'd mind more than I do."; else C18's question if nobody asked it, or her reply; on `taken` her reply is "Eleven years she paid my mum's care home. Then she sold me to you."; then "I liked you. I'd still have pressed it."); a wrongly accused officer not recalled; Voltage; Gary, or Imran when Gary isn't there (protected Gary on `imran_outcome == "ko"`: "I sat with Imran till the medics came. He asked who it was. I said I didn't see."); Wendell; Victoria in custody.
- **The numbers.** Netherton comes down the line of vans: "[What did it cost, sir?]" / "[(Let him talk.)]"; on lethal, "He reached. I will put that first." first (ruling 19). The head start by `head_start_out` ("Two cells have it. We will be finding out which entries for a year." / "Zero Day got its two hours. Nobody else did." / "Zero Day got nothing. The resale went out at four." / "The cells got nothing at four."), the bait's catch ("The resale's buyers paid for nothing. Five of them, and we have their names.") and the trace. Then "[And the rest of it?]" / "[(Wait.)]", offered only when one of these has a line (R3 A3-02): Social Fabric's story, "Someone in the second van has bruises ENTROPY did not give them. I will want that explained." (any raid member knocked out, naming nobody), "The Zero Day tap went dark at five. She'll have sold that too." (`confronted`), the $12.8M. **No longer said at 06:00** (R2 A2-01): the partial release, the backdoor, "Seattle's went first." (the core said them; ELSEWHERE carries Seattle). Then "[And the database?]" / "[(Nod.)]" (`executed`: "[(Say nothing.)]" alone).
- **The database's beat.** Secured: "His copy goes into the vault tonight, beside ours." / HaX "Two maps now." (not on `executed`), then Netherton's line for `close_secure_cell` (for example "Sterling's watch paid out at five. Zero Day's UK desk is being picked up as we speak."). Disclosed: HaX's acknowledgements line (not on `executed`), Netherton's two "go dark" lines (below), the water utilities (HaX's line; Netherton's on `executed`). Both wiped: the suspension (§4.5 table) closes 06:00 and the hearing follows.
- **The van, the forty-seven and the rest.** As the table above. Before the forty-seven, HaX on a Kowalczyk nobody named: "Kowalczyk came through his door for the key. Nobody named her before she did it herself." (when not visited at the van) or "Kowalczyk's awake in the second van. She hasn't asked who put her there." (knocked out, never named). `kept`: "Now. The forty-seven. Tell me properly, and then it's done." `denied`: "The forty-seven. You told me she was fishing. Now tell me what she actually said." and, after, "Don't do that to me again." The rest opens on "[You said you'd tell me the rest.]" (m09 played) or "[(Stand with her.)]"; the screen turns over to six between its two lines. Two phone texts, unvoiced, sent during the cutscene and held by the engine until it closes, so they arrive in the thread after it (m10 S5-6; texts sent during a person-chat wait for its close): Ghost's message (by branch; on the kept and refused branches it answers the player's reply to Ghost's question), and the Recruiter's line on `double_crossed` ("I'll remember the shape of you."), sent from a hidden `recruiter_phone` contact on the phone's `npcIds` (milestone S4–5 A12). (The narrator line for a knocked-out Netherton, "Netherton has a dressing over one eye. Nobody asks.", is withdrawn: he is `koImmune` in m10, m10 RL §2.1.) The word "afterwards" is said at most twice in a run: his line in exchange 3, and HaX's "It's afterwards." on arrest; on Scorched Earth his card is its only use.

**Every promised payoff has a home** (m10 change 33): each person met tonight gets an outcome clause in the credits' "In this operation", in the order the player met them, stating an outcome and never the choice that caused it ("Imran Siddiqui, the workshop. Went home. So did a friend."); Elena Torres, the forty-seven, Albion's inquiry (with m09's half on the notice), the m07 leaders' cells and anything else with nobody in m10 to carry it get one clause each in the credits' **"Elsewhere"** block (m10 change 41), except the one the card's human line already carries; dispatch, which always takes the card when it applies, is on the card only (m10 S6 §3.5). m09's clauses (m09 MC ch. 6, m10 MC §10): `m09_legacy_files_saved`, "Eleven placements outside SAFETYNET, picked up from the Helena files."; the Recruiter's clause on every m09 value (below: "rolled up at six", "about half of it. She warned the rest.", or "at large, her people at their desks"; MS-04); and on `m09_recruiter_secret = told`, a clause in the player's own "In this operation" line, never in "Elsewhere": "Told their handler about the Recruiter's call." (m10 S6 §3.3) Nightshade's fate is his "In this operation" clause (taken in after a shown file: "Held the outer lock. Gone by morning, under a new name."); a shown Nightshade left at the post also has his fixed 06:00 "stay" line (§4.6).

| Ending | Tesseract | HaX | Netherton | Lines that change |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Arrest (F13: a public trial, no cells) | Holds out his wrists before he is asked: "A courtroom, then. I did cost it." On the hold-fire arrest (`fire_held`, fewer than two points, the player holds while HaX kills the feed: "Got it. Feed's dead. Hold. *breath* He's done."), he takes his hand off the key, says "Ninety-nine.", then holds out his wrists. A re-talk after: "I'll see you in court, Agent." At 06:00, in a police van; he nods to HaX through the window, and has the chapter exchange with Netherton ("I wrote that chapter, Magnus." / "I know. I kept it to one page."). A taken-in Nightshade gets his look at the van window ("You wrote me as a line item." / "You were a good one."). Credits: "Held out his wrists before anyone asked." (the card carries the trial) | "You'll want a lawyer, Adrian. Get a good one. I'm testifying." As the van goes, "It's afterwards.", which opens the rest; the rest's last line, "I said no. They'll work out who you are by Thursday. Be careful." (a re-talk: "Thursday, Agent. Remember."); the chapter exchange through the van window; a taken-in Nightshade's look, as on arrest. (full form, ruling 19) | Quieter than any other success. ENTROPY has a martyr; the cells go quiet, which is worse. A review board is named for after the credits. |
