Quintero comes in from the lock and takes the weapon out of the player's hand without a word (two of the van team when she is wounded, knocked out or sent back). | Silence, then the designation once, "0x00."; no van, no rest; a message later (§4.5). | Refuses to sign: "I will not put my name under this one. You will be held until the board sits."; then the numbers and the database's beat, and "Take 0x00 to the second van. Nobody discusses tonight with them until the board sits." (§4.5). | The story with the one true paragraph (§4.5); the card reads "shot unarmed". |
| Scorched Earth | Gone by the service door (on `m09_sleeper_named`, nobody propped it, so by a panel nobody had found). A card on the console, his hand: "Afterwards, then." | At the core: "Out, 0x00. The team's at the lock. Follow their torches." Stands with the player at the hearing two days later ("He still knows. But the map's gone. He's one man with a memory now."), takes the forty-seven there on `kept` / `denied`, tells the rest, and her last word is about the player, by `hax_asked_database` (stage 7 R2–R3): asked, "They'll ask me if I'd have stopped you. I'll tell them you asked, and I said it was yours."; not asked, "... I'll tell them I wasn't asked." (m10 stage 2 §3.1, m10 MC §8.2). | Suspends the player at 06:00: "Agent 0x00. As of now you are suspended, pending a hearing." / "I wanted to do the same. That is why you are suspended and I am not." / "Nobody holds the map now. Not him, not the buyers, not whoever sits in my chair next." | The service rebuilds its threat catalogue from nothing. The teaser slot, if used, sits after the suspension. |
| Join ENTROPY | "*quietly* Thank you, Agent." Every screen reads RELEASED; he takes his coat and holds the service door for the player. | The Citadel scene, "an hour later", opens on the wall screen: Netherton at the forward post's folding table in Montana, HaX's channel open beside him, nobody speaking on it (stage 7 R1 A-04; ruling 9, from stage 8 CR: Netherton spent the night at the forward post, so he can't be at the Citadel in person an hour later). Her last message, unanswered: the player's designation, and nothing else, m10 text T-57 ("0x00.", on `ending` set to `joined`). No rest. Her SAFETYNET credit in `safetynet_join`: "Handler. Her last message to 0x00 went unanswered." (ruling 9, chosen by the user; it replaces "Her desk was empty by morning.", which brought back the image A-04 cut). | On the wall link from Montana (ruling 9; his two lines stand as written): "He told her it had to be one of ours. He was right, and I signed for that agent myself." Then, as the casualty count climbs: "Find 0x00. Bring them in, and bring them to me." | No 06:00 scene; the casualty curve on the Citadel's screen, climbing (Join's frame is the Citadel; MS-15). The after-ending lines elsewhere (`m10_hax_post.ink`, HaX's phone, Netherton's resting knot) are gated `ending != "joined"` (ruling 9, from stage 8 VT). Join's card is three lines: "Dr Adrian Tesseract: free." / "The database: everywhere." / "Every cell has it." (m10 S6 R2-10); its credits end on "Zero Day: still trading." |

**The database choice in the epilogue** (R3-05; m10 MC §6.5, §8.2, P3, P30). Each option gets a scene beat with a consequence, not only a name on the card:
- **Secured:** Netherton files the stolen copy in the vault beside the master ("Two maps now," HaX says). The first of the two named operations (§4.1 step 4's priority list) rolls up a cell on screen before the credits, through the cells ledger. The card notes "The database: back in the vault. Infusion pumps in 140 hospitals are still unpatched." On `m06_assets_seized` the Council keeps the $12.8M with the copy, as evidence (row 80).
- **Disclosed:** HaX reads the acknowledgements, at disclosure's true speed: "Forty vendors have acknowledged. Patch windows, ninety days. It'll be a year before most of them are fitted." (not on the cold ending, where she reads nothing). The pumps' vendor has a date; on Austin-uncovered saves the dispatch vendor ships a workaround within days and the fix on the ninety-day window. Both named operations go dark, said by Netherton at 06:00 in the core's list order (stage 7): "Sterling's line went dead at five." / "Nightshade has been moved. I cannot reach him." / "The HashChain tags dropped at five past." / "The Critical Mass watch went blind at five." / "The order-book watch went quiet with the first patches." / "Our tap on Zero Day's brokers dropped at ten past five.", the first two that apply. And the public cost: "Three water utilities went to manual at five. There's no patch for their controllers. They'll turn the valves by hand till spring."
- **Both wiped:** Scorched Earth's own beats (above): the wipe's gain line, the flaws still out there, unmapped, and his memory as the only map.

**The head start** (R2-08, R3-02; `head_start_out`). Both shares stopped: "The cells got nothing." Otherwise by branch: Zero Day's share killed at the console or stopped in the gallery (Victoria confronted or trusted with the share dead, escaped Victoria talked down or let walk, or Vos using arrested Victoria's information), Zero Day got nothing; Ghost's buyers killed at the console (the bait's catch: "Five buyers, and we have their names."), or Ghost talked out of the sale, deal kept, or parley, Ghost sold nothing. Otherwise: "Two cells have it. We'll be finding out which entries for a year." The trace's line plays when Zero Day's share went out traced.

**The cells ledger: one fixed rule** (milestone ruling, D-05's simplification list; replaces m10 MC P33 and P52's seven sources and fallback chain). Applied once when `ending` is set. Each source names its own cell, and no two sources can name the same one, so nothing needs a fallback:
- **The bait or the trace: one.** The bait catch takes Ghost's buyers; the trace takes Zero Day's buyers (when the share went out traced). Only one of the two can happen, since the console does one thing.
- **Secure: one**, the first named operation's own cell (Victoria's watch: Zero Day's UK desk; Nightshade's channel: the Initiative's procurement desk; the HashChain tags: the Crypto Anarchists' reserve; the Critical Mass watch: Critical Mass; the order-book watch: the book's largest buyer; the Zero Day tap: Zero Day's brokers).
- **Surrender: two**, the two cells his protocol reaches, which no other source names: the cells' couriers and their quartermaster.
- **m07: by m07's rule** (below, with `m07_completed`).

A taken-in Imran's front supplier and arrested Victoria's UK book are no longer ledger cells; each is that person's own "In this operation" clause. The Recruiter's network on `named` is her "Elsewhere" clause (below), not a cell. The card gives the count and names one cell, the first that applies of surrender's (the couriers), secure's, the console's (the bait's or the trace's buyers) and m07's, in that order (m10 S6 §3.2, computed as `close_cell_name`); the credits list them all once.

**The closing card** (m10 change 33; m10 MC §10, P10; **as written at stage 6**, m10 S6 §3.1–3.2, S6-13). Five short lines in this order, each a different shape, arriving after the scenes have said most of it; each group's conditions are exclusive, so exactly one line of each of the first four holds and the engine's five-line cap never drops one:
1. **The Architect, a headline** (one line per ending): "Dr Adrian Tesseract: in a cell, awaiting trial." / "...: cooperating. No trial." / "...: dead at his console, reaching for the key." / "...: shot unarmed." / "...: gone, with everything he remembers."
2. **The database, a headline**: "The database: back in the vault. Infusion pumps in 140 hospitals are still unpatched." (`secured`); "The database: with the vendors. Forty have answered. The fixes are ninety days out." (`disclosed`; the only "ninety days" in the close); on `both_wiped`, split on `head_start_out` (m10 S6 R1C-M3): "The database: gone, both copies. Nobody holds the map." only where nothing went at four, else "The database: gone, both copies. The cells kept what they took at four."
3. **The head start, in his own arrival words** (§4.1 step 0), so the card answers him: "Nothing went at four." / "Zero Day has it." (+ " We followed it." when traced) / "Someone's buyers have it. Not Zero Day's." / "Two cells have it." (+ " We followed Zero Day's."). "Ghost" never appears on the card.
4. **The cells, a sentence**, name first and count after, from the ledger below: "Not one cell broken." / "<name> broken. That was all." / "<name> broken, and <one…four> more." (for example "Zero Day's UK desk broken, and one more."). The count includes m07's covered cell, so the line never says "tonight"; the couriers and the buyers are never called cells on the card, and the buyers carry no number there ("four buyers" is only in THE CELLS). The cold ending counts a secured cell like any other (m10 S6-24).
5. **One human line, always a person**, the first that applies (§13 item 36 settled; order changed by m10 S6-11): (a) **dispatch**, Austin uncovered: "Callers in eleven counties will get through again by the weekend." on `disclosed`, else "In eleven counties, people calling for help still get a dead line. Day thirteen."; (b) **Albion's inquiry**, one sentence for m04 and m09's notice (row 59); (c) **the forty-seven**, only while some were not reached in time: "Of the forty-seven, twenty-eight were warned in time. Nineteen had already signed." or "None of the forty-seven were warned. They're still on someone's list."; (d) **Elena Torres**, "Elena Torres is in treatment. It's working."; (e) otherwise **the telescope**: "In Billings, a girl turns nine on Saturday. Her father is bringing a telescope." (m10 S6 R2-03), so the card ends on a person on every save.

A full example (arrest, secured, nothing out at four, Zero Day's desk, Austin uncovered): "Dr Adrian Tesseract: in a cell, awaiting trial." / "The database: back in the vault. Infusion pumps in 140 hospitals are still unpatched." / "Nothing went at four." / "Zero Day's UK desk broken, and one more." / "In eleven counties, people calling for help still get a dead line. Day thirteen." Join's card is three lines: "Dr Adrian Tesseract: free." / "The database: everywhere." / "Every cell has it." (m10 S6 §3.6, R2-10).

**`close_compute`: no `||` anywhere in the close** (coordinator ruling at m10 stage 6; m10 S6 §3.1, S6-23). Card and credits conditions, like object variants, take `globalVars.<name>`, `!`, `===`, `!==` and `&&` only: the engine's evaluator has no `||` and no parentheses, and a condition written with `||` is silently always false. Where the close needs an "or" or a count, one ink function, `close_compute` in `m10_carryover_vars.ink`, runs on the first line of each closing scene (`six_oclock`, `hearing`, `citadel`) and writes five string globals by plain ink `~` assignments to synced global `VAR`s (no tags inside the function, m10 S6 R2-06): `close_secure_cell` (the secured operation's cell, or `""`), `close_cell_name`, `close_cells` (`c0`…`c5`), `close_human` (the human line's value, `none` for the telescope) and `close_zero_day` (`vos_named` / `trading`). Every value starts with a letter (ink turns digit-only strings into numbers); defaults `""`; all three legs declared. A person's "either of these" is two credit clauses with the same text; a line's is two exclusive rows with the same text. The card and credits read single `===` tests, and the ERB helper `cells_card_lines` writes the cells line's (count, name) pairs as fixed strings.

**The credits** (m10 S6 §3.3–3.6). Title line(s); "FULL DISCLOSURE"; then IN THIS OPERATION, one consequence per person with a detail only that person has, the player first and Tesseract last; Tesseract's credit never repeats his card line (arrest "Held out his wrists before anyone asked."; surrender "Typed the cells' protocol himself, without hurrying."; lethal "His key never went down."; executed "Hands empty, in plain sight."; m10 S6 R2-01); the player is "0x00" throughout and the custody and suspension sit in the titles only. THE CELLS lists every cell in the ledger once, leaders by name. **ELSEWHERE runs in three movements** (m10 S6 §3.5, W-05): what is still out there (the Recruiter, Graham Reeves, Hollis, Static, Vance, Social Fabric's story, ending on Zero Day: "Zero Day: still trading." or "Zero Day: still trading. Its broker, Pieter Vos, named."); what the night did to systems (Seattle's batch, the water utilities, Albion and Carrow Controls, the legacy files); then people (the forty-seven, Elena Torres, and last the telescope, guarded `close_human !== 'none'` so it never prints twice). No ELSEWHERE line repeats the card's human line, and its last line is always a person. **Join** has no THE CELLS and only movement 1, so its credits end on "Zero Day: still trading." (W-21). The season's last lines, the telescope and Join's ending, are taste calls put to the user (m10 S6 §7).

**SAFETYNET and IN MEMORY** (stage 7, `scenarios/m10_full_disclosure/credits_sections.json`; m10 contract §5.9). Two more `section` blocks, placed by stage 9 after ELSEWHERE and before the production credits: `safetynet` in E1–E5, `safetynet_join` in its place in E6, `in_memory` in all six. SAFETYNET lists, in capitals and by designation, Netherton, HaX, Quintero ("AGENT 0x2D 'ANCHOR'"), Arakawa, Malhotra, Vector and Phantom on `vector_on_team` / `phantom_on_team`, and last "HALINA KOWALCZYK, 'WREN'" in the warning style, her Initiative codename under SAFETYNET's heading. **Kowalczyk's clauses**, first that holds: on `m09_recruiter_offer === 'double_crossed'`, "Former breaching lead. Walked off the apron in Helena. Taken at the core."; on `sleeper_caught`, "Breaching lead, Task Force Null. Eleven years in SAFETYNET. Badge returned."; on `wren_at_core`, "... Stopped at the core with her hand out for his key."; on `kowalczyk_ko`, "... Woke in the second van. Nobody had named her."; otherwise "... Badge still active." (never her only clause, R1 A-02). The "..." on the last three is the same opening, "Breaching lead, Task Force Null. Eleven years in SAFETYNET.": the task force dates from m04's debrief (m05: "three people and a whiteboard"), and her eleven years are service (ruling 8, from stage 8 CC 1 and SR major 3). HaX's Join clause: see §4.5's Join row (ruling 9). Quintero's first clause on `quintero_ko` is "Raid lead. On her feet by Friday, and asking questions."; HaX's and Netherton's go by ending. IN MEMORY is two unconditional lines, "Agent 0x19 Callum Fraser." and "Agent 0x6B Ana Lúcia Ferreira.". m09's own credits list Kowalczyk by her hangar reply ("Thanked for her cracker, to her face." / "Asked after her cracker. Got a shrug.") or, on `double_crossed`, "Took a phone call on the apron and walked away." (`texts/C_texts_and_credits.md`).

**The Recruiter and her network** (R2-20, C2, C14; milestone MS-04). One rule for her state, and one "Elsewhere" clause for every value, so each m09 route has a home at the close. She has no line of her own on the card.
- `named`: her network rolled up, from 06:00 on day 13 (the arrests held so WREN wouldn't hear, D-01): "The Recruiter's network: rolled up at six."
- `double_crossed`: about half of it: "The Recruiter's network: about half of it. She warned the rest." (m09 change 4; m10 MC §6.2 moved it off the card; wording m10 S6 §3.5).
- `taken` or `refused` (her file wiped): she stays at large and her network in place: "The Recruiter: at large, her people at their desks."
- `""` (m09 not played): no clause. What "her network" means depends on m05: the twenty-three placements by default ("Eleven are still at their desks" when it stays in place); on `public_exposure` the twenty-three were blown in m05, so it is her handlers and the forty-seven; on `turn_double_agent` it is the names Torres couldn't give.

The closing card's cells line, and the "Elsewhere" block, also cover the cells the finale doesn't stage, by branch (row 103), with `m07_completed`: Michael Reeves's cell and Marcus Ashford's cell by whether Fracture and Meltdown were covered in m07; Jennifer Walsh's cell by Austin. **On the closing screen each cell is named by its leader, never by its cell name**, so "Ghost Protocol" never sits beside Ghost's 06:00 text (name clash; orchestrator ruling); Reeves keeps his first name (row 21). Never played m07: the card follows m07's never-played canon (§3): Austin reached, the other sites not. Then Zero Day, and the Recruiter by the rule above.

## 5. Cast

Sprite keys are the ones the shipped missions use (inventories A §2, B §m0N.2). New characters need sprites and the usual character pipeline at stage 9.

**Speaker names and how people address the player** (stage 7; m09 contract §8.3, m10 contract §1.2 and R1 R-1). Each ink prefix is the NPC's `displayName`, character for character: "Agent HaX", "Director Magnus Netherton", "Agent 0x5E 'Lantern'" (Kowalczyk), "Agent 0x2D 'Anchor'" (Quintero), "Officer Kenji Arakawa", "Officer Dev Malhotra", "Agent 0x47 'Nightshade'", "Margaret Sorensen", "Noor Haddad", "Jesse Tran", "Cody Old Coyote", "Lyle Dillard", "Dr Adrian Tesseract (recording)" in m09, and "Dr Adrian Tesseract", "Sione Fonoti", "Imran Siddiqui", "Wendell Briggs", "Dr Abena Asante", "Pieter Vos", "Minder", "Guard" in m10; returning characters keep their own mission's form ("Dr. James Mercer", "Voltage (Critical Mass)" pending stage 9's choice). **Nobody uses a personal name or a gendered word for the player.** In m10, SAFETYNET people (HaX, Netherton and the raid team) and Tesseract may say "Agent", "Agent 0x00" or "0x00" aloud; nobody else names the player at all. In m09 the Recruiter is the exception and stands: her "Agent 0x00…" and "Goodnight, agent." match how she spoke in m05 (ruling 18, from stage 8 CC 2; ink unchanged). This replaces m10 stage 7a's rule that only HaX and Tesseract could say "0x00" or "Agent" (R1 R-1: the voice bible has Netherton open with the agent's designation, and m08's Netherton says "Agent" throughout). Netherton speaks in full forms on weighted lines and uses contractions only in quick asides (R-2). The credits and every written line call the player "0x00".

Villanueva is the next smear target on the last page of the Social Fabric letters, warned if the player saves that bin (m09 MC §3.1; grepped by the m09 author, no clash). Farah is Imran's friend, sent home when he is talked round (Imran rings her: "I've rung Farah."; a bark, "Where's Farah? She was on till six.", and her coat on the chair). **Stage 7 puts her on the map when she stays**: she is the walkway guard `farah_guard` (`m10_guards.ink` knot `farah`), speaker "Guard:" with her name on the rota only, who challenges the player ("Who are you? This walkway's closed till six.") and turns hostile on either answer; so the talk-round removes one fight from the gallery, and taking Imran in leaves it there. Abara (the tunnel roster); Gallatin Instrument Supply of Bozeman is the bench's front supplier (m10 S6 §2.4). (Social Fabric's media crew is cut with the media room.) |

### 5.3 Default occupants

Every room that a carry-over can fill has a default occupant, so the route works with all imports at their defaults.

m10 has eight playable rooms (D-16; the media room is cut, milestone D-05): forward post (with the gate in view), blast-door tunnel, security office (the gatehouse merged in), workshop floor, power room, server hall, gallery, core (with the host terminal). The service tunnel is a cutscene. Stage 5 laid them out as one corridor (m10 RL §1): forward post 4×2 (start) → blast tunnel 2×1 → security office 2×2 → (stair, south) workshop floor 2×2 → power room 2×2 → server hall 4×2 → gallery 2×1 → core 2×2, every door east–west or between rooms of equal width, so every pair aligns. Three new maps (forward post, gallery walkway, core) and a dressed or new server hall; bunker props first (m10 S5-7, S5-9, S5-16). **Every holder of a forced scene is `koImmune`** (S5-8); m09's hatch and door-scene copies of Sorensen are too (m09 S4 P3).

**The five full-scene slots** (R2-01). Each is on the main path (§4.0) and holds a person on every combination of choices; a won branch and a lost branch put different people there.

| Slot | Won or careful branch | Lost or careless branch | Default (never played, or neither) |
|---|---|---|---|
| The door (forward post) | Nightshade arrested (`and not m08_nightshade_ko_before_fate`): in cuffs; refuse him again (first) or the deal. Triple agent, shown his file: the override (the alarm panel), his channel spent; stay at the post or come and hold the outer lock | Triple agent, not shown: his map or Helena's plans; his map trips the wire unless the payment is checked | Nightshade in the van: "Door."; Netherton's tether question, bring him to the blast door ("Manual.") or leave him (m10 change 23). Every branch: one closing choice at the table, `door_scene_done` |
| The workshop | Gary protected: at the door; Imran Siddiqui talked round (home with his phone; Farah home) or taken in (the supplier) | Gary unprotected and quiet: on the floor, recruited, beside Imran, who follows him; talked round, left in place, or cuffed | Imran Siddiqui, with a cloneable card: talk him round, take him in, clone the card, or knock him out; or the fitter's spare card from the security office's locker (m10 S5-10); the bench log (clue 3) |
| The power room | Voltage captured: placed at the power room after the breach; his hands (his credit clause) or a hand cut he talks the player through (the camera switch) | Voltage escaped: at the switchgear (with Static if he left with him); proved, talked down, or fought | Wendell Briggs at the panel: tell him (Dale stays home; the server hall is warned), or work round him (the camera switch); Vance, Elena or HaX on the phone |
| The gallery | Victoria arrested: her optional secure-line call in the server hall, then Vos face to face in the gallery, her information used automatically. Recruited: confronted (holds; costs the tap) or trusted with Zero Day's share dead (holds; keeps both); refused if never answered. Escaped without the threat: her trade for walking out. Escaped with it and talked down: she cancels the order | Recruited and trusted while the share is live: sells the raid. Escaped with the threat: stand-off, and a miss leaves the knock-out | Pieter Vos, with his code-card trade: buy or refuse (D-04: two options per branch) |

Refused: Ghost's question back, whose answer chooses the gift (the lock's code or the code card's holder), no sale | Deal broken: the sale, harder to stop (own the broken deal first). Unanswered: the sale, and "Answer this." Talk Ghost out, or bait the sale | Ghost as a stranger, selling |

### 5.4 HaX's arc

m08 left her close to coming apart, texting "Same classroom, 0x00. Don't ask me whose yet." The finale is her reckoning with the man who taught her.

- **m09.** In person at the briefing, then on the line. At the briefing she holds the name back; on `m06_architect_identity_found` she reminds the player, alone, that she called it Satoshi's guess. On the Recruiter's call she prices every route aloud (her boast, "I can beat that wipe.", and the invoice as a moral cost), and on the sold-and-lied branch she hears the player own it, deny it or hang up; on `told`, after the deck: "Back in September I'd have kept that between us. Not tonight." (was "Last spring"; m05 is September 2026, ruling 13) At the notice she says what is exposed, what the watch is and what sending costs, and refuses to decide ("It isn't my call. It isn't yours either."). After the deck she rings unasked, names what the purge cost (which bin and which tape are gone; both tapes on `taken`), and gives three words and one sentence: "Afterwards. Not during." / "It's yours to carry. Nobody else signed for it."; then, as the beat's last line, "The rest when this is over." m09 never says what the rest is; m10 pays it (m09 change 15). In the identity scene (heard in the salvage cage beside Tomb Beta's server, by phone and voiced, Netherton listening from the aircraft; m09 S4 P1, F17) she says the name as fact for the first time; her first line is the recognition line, if any (row 97 or row 37). The four forms are labelled by import (row 70). **Every form reaches the wall**: she had the name the night Fraser and Ferreira died, and she didn't say it. **The player answers her** (R2-19): m06 name not found, "You should have told me." / "I'd have done the same in your place."; m06 name found, "You should have taken it to Netherton." / "I'd have sat on it too." (m09 change 19); on every form, a third answer, her own line back to her, "Afterwards. Not during." (`afterwards`, m09 change 24). No answer is wrong; the answer is exported (`hax_confession_answer`). The Reading has one line about her ("Their handler will cover for them. She was taught to."), and her reply knows it if the player told her. At the debrief, last on the line, by answer: `should_have_told`, she tells the player everything from now on; `same_in_your_place`, a thank-you, once; `afterwards`, "You said afterwards. I'm holding you to it."; and the rest is still owed. On `m08_hax_ko` her first briefing line is "You put me on the floor at the Citadel. I'm still running you." (stage 7: the briefing's opening, ahead of "Keep not asking. By morning you won't need to." and the plain "Sorry about the leave. This is why."; m10 pays the same knock-out through Tesseract, §3.6). At the end Tesseract rings her, not the player, and calls her "Ninety-nine", as on the course; she refuses it: "It's HaX. It has been for seven years." (m09 change 13). The player hears her side only, then "I'll bring them." She reports it in four short sentences: he called her Ninety-nine; the blast door will open for 0x00 by name; take your time getting there; and he asked whether they had liked his notes.
- **m10.** At the forward post with Netherton, on the line all night; her answer to the optional "[How are you?]" in her hub (from the briefing until flag 2, asked once; not a briefing line, stage 8 SA m2) follows `m09_hax_confession_answer` (on `afterwards`: "You gave me my own line back in Helena. I'm holding you to it."). She knows the bunker's tricks because she knows his. Tesseract's jamming drops her line for one short, linear stretch (the gallery: one room, one obvious door). Nothing rings there, the phone stays usable and her hub opens on static; she is back the moment the player steps down into the server hall, and for good once the outer lock opens (m10 RL §6 item 33, OT §1). Her last line before the drop names what to do next and carries every warning the gallery needs (D-22; m10 MC §3.5, P2): on recruited Victoria, "Sterling goes with whoever's winning. Make sure it's us before you hand her anything. Show her the order, and she'll know we read Zero Day's mail." That is the only branch warning it carries now (milestone D-04): arrested Victoria's information is used automatically, so its use-or-keep line is gone, and the sweep line, "Netherton sweeps the whole place at six. Whoever you leave standing up there, he collects.", moves to the server hall's exit, said on every branch as the player leaves for the gallery stair. As flag 4 lands she gives the player the one fact about the console ("I can try to kill the feed. Try.") and the purge warning, repeated at the database menu (P5); at the menu she refuses to choose for the player. At the core he greets her, "Ninety-nine. You've done well with them.", and she answers "Still HaX." on every branch. He talks to her through the player's handset; she does not decide for the player and says so. On `same_in_your_place` or `afterwards`, "[HaX. You're on speaker.]" is one of the player's answers in the third exchange: the player hands her the floor, and she makes the argument only she can make, as his student (on `afterwards` she first says "All right. Let him hear it from me."; m10 change 38). On `should_have_told` she speaks once at the core, unasked: "My agent told me I sat on it. They were right. You never told me anything." That puts a different answer under the third claim ("She learned that from you."). No answer to her confession is the wrong one. Her one act on the failed talk-down (killing the feed when his hand goes to the console) stays. On `m09_recruiter_secret = kept`, Tesseract tells her on the open line that her agent never explained the receipt; she answers "I'll deal with that when this is done.", and does, in a fixed beat at 06:00 that plays whatever the player picks in the walkabout (§4.6). On `denied` the lie is exposed in front of him ("You told me she was fishing."), she doesn't look at the player again until the database menu, and the 06:00 beat is a harder conversation. At 06:00 she pays m09's promise, "the rest", in two lines: "The night he left, he asked me to go with him." / "I said no. It took me till morning." (m10 changes 21, 39). "Afterwards" is said at most twice in a run.
- **Endings.** §4.6. In every success ending except the cold one she gets the last word before the card, and tells the rest; on executed and Join she does not. On the double-cross branch she calls Quintero in at the core ("Quintero. Core. Now."); on `alpha_tapes` she warns about the service door and spares Quintero.

### 5.5 Netherton's role

- **m09.** Runs the briefing in Task Force Null's room (a cutscene): the order, which SAFETYNET read and let go on ("We read it. Then we let it go on. If it hadn't arrived, he would know."), how it was caught (one reason by priority), the wall of cards as the "previously on", the suspect without the name ("I will not say the name in this room until we can prove it."), the raid team introduced. (The stage 0 "legal cover" line on Victoria's CPS case was never written; her case is HaX's wall card, row 34.) On `m08_netherton_ko` or `m08_netherton_hostile`, a narrator line: he takes the chair furthest from the player's, and nobody mentions why (m09 stage 1, W-15). Says the name aloud for the first time when the proof comes together, from the aircraft's forward cabin with the door shut, in words that work whether or not the player heard his m08 pledge, with one sentence on who Tesseract was, then "HaX." and silence while she confesses (row 125; m09 change 7). The debrief is a cutscene in a hangar at Helena's airfield, Netherton in person: the hour ("Twenty hours. He has given us every one of them."); Kowalczyk's "Did my toy earn its keep?" (on `double_crossed` she isn't there, and Netherton reaches the player first: "Kowalczyk took a phone call when we landed and walked off the apron. Her phone was in a bin by the fuel bowser."); the player's stance on Tesseract's memo ("Was he right?"); then, if the notice is still unsent, he hands it to the player by m04 branch ("You read it. You decide. I will sign whichever.", full form by ruling 19; m09 MC §3.5, ch. 19); a question hub (the archive, at most two lines; WREN; the Recruiter; the notice, only if sent in the archive); he reads the two dead agents' names; he signs the Montana order in front of the player (on `m08_debrief_stance = owned`, with his own name beside Nightshade's file; on `defended`, with one quiet thank-you he doesn't explain) and answers the stance at the pen ("Then I sign for him too." / "Good. Then he can make that argument in court, and lose it."); last, why the raid team goes in together, WREN and all (row 117; on `double_crossed`, "She knows we know. She will be where he is. So will we.", ruling 19); he caps the pen (on `double_crossed`: "He caps the pen. Her crate is still on the ramp."). On `m08_netherton_ko` he says it once, at the pen: "You put me on the floor at the Citadel. Nobody has mentioned it. I am mentioning it once." (stage 7 R3 I-7; guarded by the ink-local `said_floor_line`, so a reload mid-spine doesn't repeat it). In the briefing, at the wall's last two cards (a photograph each, nothing written), the player can ask "[Who are the last two?]" before his "Later." (R3 C-R3-02).
- **m10.** Commands the raid from the forward post; may call the player "Agent", "Agent 0x00" or "0x00", never anything personal (§5 intro). Runs the briefing and the private aside (§4.0; on `double_crossed`, the empty place and "Kowalczyk walked off the apron in Helena. We know where she has gone."; at the aside, "Whoever it is wants the same room you do. Find them before you get there."), answers Quintero's objection, and spends his handbook line there. At the door he signs for whatever the player decides about Nightshade, and on the silent branch he holds the tether question. On `m07_debrief_stance = hardened` he tells the player the Council said yes, and the projection desk is the result (row 99). Holds whoever the player accuses and recalls them over HaX's channel. At the database choice he and Tesseract name each other once ("Magnus." / "Adrian."); he makes secure's public-safety case, the one argument that is his to make, names the two live operations disclosure would burn and why (they ride holes on the list; §4.1), states no preference, and authorises disclosure anyway if the player chooses it. Before the end-for-him menu on two or more points he says what F13 means: "If he cooperates, there is no trial. He gives us cells, and nobody hears his speech. Arrest him, and he makes it in open court." At 06:00, his lines with numbers (tonight's cost, the trace, the $12.8M), and the chapter exchange through the van window on arrest and surrender. Refuses to sign on the executed ending. Suspends the player on Scorched Earth. Orders the player brought in on Join. His m08 line "If he burns you, it is on my signature" comes due at the door on the triple-agent branch either way.

## 6. VM flags in story terms

The VM plan is written separately (`finale/FINALE_VM_PLAN.md`); it decides how each flag is reached and must fit the meanings below. Every flag is required (`concludeRequires`, the four flag tasks only in both missions), and is followed by a HaX line that says what it bought in story terms. Wiring, as stages 4–5 settled it: a flag carries one reward; no flag reward opens a door (story doors open by HaX's `unlockDoor` mappings keyed on the flag's global, F19), and the F18 fix, which makes a reward's `unlock_door` open its own target room, is in the engine but used by neither mission. m09's two stations reward `set_global flagN_submitted`; m10's four stations each list only their own flag and reward `emit_event` (`flag_N_in`), on which HaX's mapping sets `flagN_submitted` (m10 S4-21, RL §2). A wrong-station flag gets a hint naming the right terminal.

### 6.1 m09: the archive's servers

Two separate machines: the archive's catalogue server (flags 1–2) and the old Tomb Beta server, which stands in the **salvage cage**, a small mesh pen off the tape room (flags 3–4; m09 S4 P1, P22). **Flags 1–2 are submitted at the SAFETYNET drop terminal** the player sets up in the reading room (the field laptop). **Flags 3–4 are submitted in the salvage cage**, beside the machine, through the field kit's link to the drop terminal; the salvage cage's station is the only one that accepts Tomb Beta's VM, and the reading room's accepts only the catalogue server's. The salvage cage is behind its own keypad, which takes the decoded catalogue code, so the decode is required: a door has one lock, and a flag station or VM launcher ignores an object lock, so the story's "card and code" needs two doors (the tape room's card reader, then the salvage cage's keypad). The identity scene, flag 4's read and Tesseract's call are all heard in the salvage cage, by phone (m09 changes 12, 22; S4 P1). The tape room keeps the Alpha-tapes deck, the shelf-code legend that keys the decode, and the cold; the salvage cage has the server's fans (P22). Ten rooms on one map (P21, P23).

| Flag | Story meaning | Unlocks in game |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **A foothold on the archive's catalogue server**: the index of every box, tape and shelf, including the restricted series the public catalogue hides. | The shelf marks for Series R, in the restricted cage; HaX's `unlockDoor` mapping on the flag opens the restricted stacks' outer door, so the cage can't come before flag 1 (m09 S4 P7); the coded catalogue line for the salvage cage's keypad (settled at stage 6, m09 S6 §2: CyberChef's **Vigenère Decode** keyed `BONEYARD`, the Salvage row of the tape room's shelf-code legend, bay 12; the line is `SALVAGE CAGE ACCESS` over `MSJMQAEGDZNVI`, the second line decoded only, giving the keypad's `LEWISANDCLARK`; HaX names the bay word and the operation), which HaX also sends to the notepad as a notes item (`catalogue_salvage_line`). The coded line is one string, identical in the notes item, on the drop terminal's screen and in HaX's quote, and free of the characters ink, ERB and JSON mangle (the full list is in §13's stage 6 handover). |
| 2 | **The Insider Threat Initiative's ledger**, kept live on the catalogue server: the twenty-three placements and the forty-seven candidates, each under a ledger codename, plus an older legacy section. WREN's legacy entry was updated on day 10: "assigned TF Null, Montana operation", with a source code "H&L" and a file number. Every player learns that much. | HaX reads it aloud. The printed ledger on the cage's ledger shelf becomes readable (optional, for the player who wants the page; it holds Reeves' handler's contact sheet on that branch and Noor's codename, LINNET; a read counts only after flag 2, as `ledger_page_seen`), and Sorensen shreds it once read if she is free (the flag holds it). The optional cross-reference opens: the Harwick & Lowe placing sheet, in a box in the open stacks that takes the ledger's file number on a keypad (P10), narrows WREN to two (`sleeper_shortlist`); the file number is `HL2015-0477`. The Recruiter rings once flag 2 is in and the cage has been opened (m09 change 21). |
| 3 | **The owner of Tomb Beta's server**: Tomb Beta ran its enrolment under a web service account, and the credential text found beside the flag is a SAFETYNET staff credential issued to Dr Adrian Tesseract before he left seven years ago, never revoked. That text is the proof of ownership; with the handwriting on Nightshade's recruitment file (matched earlier against HaX's own training notebook, with his margin notes, and confirmed by Tomb Beta's enrolment binders in the salvage cage), it proves who he is. The same machine's files hold the catalogue entry for the Albion flaw (VH-2017-0312, Carrow Controls' RM-4 module, "Disposition: RETAIN. Operational value. Review 2019.", the Chief Strategist's box on the board left empty), his memo of 14 March 2017 arguing it should go to the vendor, and the vendor notice he drafted and never sent (§1.3). | The identity scene, by phone, with HaX and Netherton (row 125). After the confession, HaX's back-to-work line opens on the leaver control nobody ran: "Seven years, and nobody turned it off." (milestone M-05; m09 SA Beat 3.3), with a field-guide pointer for the joiner-mover-leaver idea, never a lecture. Without Nightshade's file in hand, HaX holds the name until it is picked up. The "send the notice" action, through the drop terminal from HaX's hub (`albion_notified`; asked at the debrief if still unsent; P8); the Albion papers as one folder in the salvage cage, summarised by HaX (P19). On `alpha_tapes`, the clip at the tape room's reel deck once `identity_scene_done` is set (before it HaX holds it: "I know whose voice that is. After."). |
| 4 | **Full control of Tomb Beta's server**: its job that still mirrors to Tomb Gamma, carrying what the Publication is and its two modes (city by city from 06:00 UTC on day 13; everywhere in the same minute by the key at Tomb Gamma's console, which needs a hand), the 04:00 head start, the list of defences built from the stolen database (which entries he built from; not which door each is on or who stands where, so Nightshade's payment in m10 still has something to sell, ruling 6), and his original casualty sheet pricing the key, with the release configuration ("Fallback: 06:00 UTC, staged by city."), on `mirror_job_printout` (m09 changes 5, 28; m09 S6, §1.2 here). | HaX's read of the hour, a text scene, not voiced (waits on the identity scene: "His file first. Then the rest of his box."), the realisation that this server was left off the destruction schedule on purpose (said by a player who read the schedule, else stated once by HaX), Tesseract's call to HaX, the raid order, and the mission's close. |

Together with the paper finds (the deed and construction file in the lease file, the Reading, Nightshade's file), these give the four things §1.3 promises: the name, the ledger, the defences, the hour.

**The night's order and what holds it** (m09 stage 1 §11). Aims gate nothing and `concludeRequires` is the four flags only, so each step is held by a lock or a condition: flag 1 opens the restricted stacks (HaX's `unlockDoor`); the Recruiter's call waits on flag 2 **and** `cage_opened`; Sorensen's office (a keypad whose code nobody has, so the cracker can't open it early; P11) opens on `deck_choice_made` by HaX's `unlockDoor` (the Recruiter fired the wipe from the deck's controller there, and HaX traces it: "She fired it from Peg's office. I'm on the door."); the tape room's key card is in the office safe, with the Reading and the master destruction schedule; the salvage cage's keypad takes the decoded code; flags 3–4 only at the salvage cage's station; the identity scene fires on flag 3; flag 4's read waits on `identity_scene_done`; Tesseract's call waits on `hour_read_done`; the conclusion aim waits on `tesseract_call_done`. At the debrief, the notice question is asked straight after the memo stance if the notice is unsent, and the conclusion task is on the debrief's last line, after it (m09 MC §3.5; a new lock row for m09 stage 1 §11, m09 MC ch. 19). Every export is written on every route, including a finished m09 that never touched an optional find (`cage_bin` at `cage_opened`, `legacy_files_saved` at `cage_opened` or a knock-out, `albion_notified` by the debrief's question).

**One rule for every forced phone scene** (both missions; orchestrator ruling). In m09: the Recruiter's call, the identity scene, flag 4's read and Tesseract's call. Each sets its defaults on its first line (by branch, where an export depends on it), and its done-flag on its last choice, or its last line if it has no choice, never on opening or closing. Closed before that, it re-rings when the player leaves the room (an unfinished identity scene also on flag 4's submission) and resumes at its last unanswered choice; a scene with no choice restarts from its first line. The Recruiter's call has two resume points after a "yes" (m09 MC §3.2, ch. 31): closed before her naming line, she re-rings and says the name ("You hung up on the part you paid for. Halina Kowalczyk."); closed after it, the Recruiter has gone, so HaX re-rings ("I've paused the deck. It won't hold long.") at the unanswered choice. Nothing downstream keys on a conversation closing. **Built with the F14 forced-call keys** (committed in 859da19d): `disableClose` on every ring (Esc and the back arrow do nothing; only a hit or a reload ends the call), `resume: true` where the scene has a choice and the contact has no other calls (it reopens at the last unanswered choice), `cooldown: 0` on re-rings, and a `game_loaded` re-ring with the same condition beside each `room_exited` one, since nothing re-rings on load by itself (m09 S4 §6; m10 RL §6 item 32). The Recruiter's re-rings never fire as the player walks into the cage. The Recruiter's call, the identity scene and Tesseract's call are voiced (F17, F20); flag 4's read stays text. m10's ringing phone scenes follow the same rule (m10 MC rule 1.7): Ghost's handset, whose done-flag `ghost_call_done` opens the stair down (the kept branch's cupboard code is noted on the call's last line), and arrested Victoria's line, which re-rings once as the gallery stair opens, after HaX's `gallery_open` call has closed, and is abandoned with its defaults on entering the gallery (`victoria_line_done`). m10's forced face-to-face scenes are held by `koImmune` instead of KO nets (§4.0).

**HaX's forced calls re-ring to a guarded knot, not by `resume`** (milestone S4–5 ruling, M9-04, F-03). `resume` restores whatever HaX's saved story is, and she hosts several calls and a hub, so after a hit closes one of her calls the player could open her thread, answer the call's choice outside the call, and a later `resume` would land in the wrong place. Each of her forced scenes (m09: `identity_scene`, `hour_read`, `deck_resume`; m10: the by-hand cut when she is the voice, and any other call of hers with a choice) re-rings with an explicit `targetKnot`: a guarded resume knot that reads the scene's own globals and goes straight to the unanswered choice. The re-ring fires on `room_exited` and `game_loaded` as above and also on `conversation_closed:agent_0x99` with the scene's condition, so a reopened hub is followed at once by the call. Other contacts with one call each (the Recruiter, Ghost, Victoria, Vance, Elena) may keep `resume: true`, still naming a `targetKnot` for the case with no saved story.

**Voiced calls (F20, committed in 24fcb06c; `docs/agents/FINALE_ENGINE_VOICED_CALLS.md` §8).** A voiced call voices its start knots (the `targetKnot` of each `voiced: true` mapping) and every knot reachable from them, never entering the contact's home knot, which is the scenario's `currentKnot` for that contact (default `start`). So, in both missions: every voiced mapping, ring and re-ring alike, names a `targetKnot` that is not the home knot (a voiced contact with no start knot voices nothing) and carries its own `voiced: true`; every voiced knot ends `#exit_conversation` and never diverts to the home knot or the hub, so a hub topic that reuses a call knot also exits; a narrator line inside a voiced call is silent, so m09's rewrite of such lines as HaX's asides is the rule for m10 too (or the silence is kept on purpose and said so). m09's voiced calls are the Recruiter's (`call`, `resume_name`) and HaX's `identity_scene`, `tesseract_call` and `deck_resume`, about 66 lines (m09 S4 §6.7). m10's are Ghost's handset call, Victoria's secure line, the by-hand cut (Vance, Elena or HaX), and HaX's `breach_call`, `stair_open`, `inner_door`, `tripwire`, `ladder` and `gallery_open` calls, about 80 lines (m10 OT §13; was about 85 before her flag 4 lines moved into Tesseract's conversation at the m10 milestone fixes); every other HaX line on the route is a bark, unvoiced. Her flag 4 lines are her second-speaker lines inside Tesseract's conversation, voiced there as a face-to-face scene and not counted with the calls (§4.1 step 0).

**Voiced-line budgets as shipped** (stage 7 closed, 2026-10-21; distinct lines, counted by each group with `voicelines.mjs`; m09 contract §14, m10 contract §10; the orchestrator raised m09 group A's budget at R1 rather than cut carry-over payoffs). These replace the "about 66" and "about 80" estimates above.

| Mission | Scene | Lines |
|---|---|---|
| m09 | HaX's calls (`deck_resume`, `identity_scene`, `identity_resume`, `tesseract_call`) | 55 distinct, **49 new clips** (HaX 52, Netherton 3); about 35 heard per route |
| m09 | The Recruiter's call (`call`, `resume_name`) | **Recruiter 14**; HaX 17 in the call, 11 not shared with her own calls |
| m09 | Briefing cutscene | **84** character lines **+ 8** narrator |
| m09 | Debrief cutscene | **50** character lines **+ 16** narrator (R3 I-7 added Netherton's pen line) |
| m09 | The Alpha clip | 4 (Tesseract's recording 2, HaX 2) + 2 narrator |
| m09 | Elsewhere | one new Old Coyote clip, one new Sorensen clip, one new HaX bin line (R2) |
| m10 | Calls | **83** after stage 8 (81 at stage 7): HaX 29, Ghost 26, Vance 10, Elena 11, Victoria 7 |
| m10 | Tesseract's conversation (with HaX's and Netherton's second-speaker lines) | **188** after stage 8 (185 at stage 7) |
| m10 | 06:00 (`six_oclock`) | **99** |
| m10 | The hearing | **9** |
| m10 | The Citadel; Kowalczyk at the core | 2; 2 |

m10 in all has about 940 distinct voiced lines, about 300 heard per run (m10 R1). No audio is made until the dialogue is signed off (tts-audio skill), and the F20 walk of the real voiced knots is stage 9's (§21).

**The purge** (R2-10, B12, R3-04, C3, C15). It moves on story events, never on time, and it never touches what a flag yields or anything on the critical path. **One order, used in all three documents:** flag 1 → the cage → flag 2 (the ledger shelf optional) → the Recruiter's call → the tape deck.

1. **The cage.** The purge is already running when the player arrives (the order SAFETYNET let go on is why tonight): Sorensen is behind the mesh feeding the shredder from the start, reachable only by talk at the cage's hatch counter (S-09, settled at stage 5: a static-sprite record whose body reaches the counter, with a hatch bell as the fallback; §5.2). The cage gate takes her stacks card (cloned from the reel at her hip while she talks, the night's second and harder clone; the office is on its own keypad, off the card system), or, on recruited Victoria's route, the maintenance code from Sorensen's mail; her print (lifted off the stamp she leaves on the hatch shelf) opens the plan cabinet inside, which she locked under her own print (m09 S4 P2; HaX: "the gate takes her card, the cabinet takes a print"). Two bins, and HaX names what each buys, once, before any button: Derek's file (by the hatch; the Architect's hand on the player's first case, his standing order, a second sample of the hand) and, on `m08_nightshade_tidied`, the copy Nightshade sent ahead (his notes), or by default the Initiative's letters to Social Fabric, whose last page is the next smear, on Teresa Villanueva in Butte (taking them gets her warned and is a lever on Tran). Past against present (m09 MC §3.1). The player can pull one bin through the hatch; she tips the other as they do. Opening the cage is the event: by the time it opens, the other bin is gone (`cage_bin`, exported). If the player opens the cage without using the hatch, Derek's file survives and the other is gone; on Victoria's route a hidden Sorensen record (`sorensen_door`, `koImmune`) hosts the door scene on `room_entered:restricted_cage` and, like `sorensen_hatch`, holds all three bin files in its `itemsHeld` with ids (`derek_file`, `letters`, `nightshade_copy`; "[Take Nightshade's copy.]" on `m08_nightshade_tidied`) (m09 stage 5 §3.7, P3), so knocking down `sorensen` can't drop the file that went in the shredder. **Once open** (m09 MC §4.3), she keeps shredding and doesn't fight. Her last box, open on the feed table behind her, is the Initiative's legacy placement files outside SAFETYNET (the SAFETYNET files went onto the Recruiter's tape, by codename only, D-01; this box never names WREN). HaX names what and when as the cage opens: "That box behind her is the last of the night. The legacy files." then, 5 s later, "She gets to it when you pass her for the cabinet, or leave the cage. She won't stop for asking." (texts A3-06a/b; split at stage 7, A-18) It goes under the shredder at the first of Nightshade's file taken from the plan cabinet (`nightshade_file_held`) or the player first leaving the opened cage (the cage is its own room, so leaving it is an event the engine sees; P4); a knock-out on Sorensen before then saves it (`legacy_files_saved`, exported; already true if Noor held it back). An arrest alone doesn't stop her; talk does, with Nightshade's recruitment file as the lever, and gets her written account instead of the files. Optional, event-driven, no critical-path loss.
2. **Flag 2.** HaX reads the ledger; WREN is on the raid team. If the player reads the printed ledger at the shelf and Sorensen is still free, she shreds it; nothing is lost, because the flag holds it. Nightshade's recruitment file is in the locked plan cabinet at the back of the cage, on the critical path, and never purged.
3. **The tape deck** (inside the Recruiter's call). The call comes after flag 2 and the cage, so her offer never gives away a plant the ledger hasn't. The deck holds her file (a tape: her handler list, §2 row 60) and Tomb Alpha's tapes (his recorded teaching, with annotation sheets in his hand). **Her price is the whole deck** (m09 MC §3.2, ch. 1): on a take she hangs up and wipes it, and nothing comes off (`taken`, `deck_pulled = none`); on a refusal or hang-up she wipes her own file first and drops off sure it's gone, and HaX saves one tape through the drop terminal on the player's word, inside the call, her file (`named`; handlers and placements by codename only, and the arrests held until 06:00 on day 13, D-01) or the Alpha tapes (`refused`); on a double-cross HaX pulls her file after the name (`double_crossed`), and she knows something came off. There is no "pull nothing". Tran runs only the rest of the tape side (the Initiative's legacy recruitment interviews, optional lore), so stopping him early or late saves that, never the deck.

After the deck HaX rings once, unasked, and names what the purge cost: which bin and which tape are gone (both tapes on `taken`).

### 6.2 m10: Tomb Gamma's network

Four stations, one flag each (m10 RL §2, S4-13, S5-13): flag 1 at the forward post's gate terminal (worked while the pre-breach split happens around the player); flag 2 at the server hall's release console; flag 3 at the sealed section's own hatch station, at the hall's east end; flag 4 at the core's host terminal, after Tesseract has greeted 0x00 and HaX (§4.1 step 0; he pauses nothing). The core holds one flag and the conversation (R2-02); his case waits for all four flags (S4-11).

| Flag | Story meaning | Unlocks in game |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **A foothold on the security monitoring host that the camera and door systems report to.** | Camera feeds, the blast-door route, and the door log at the security office's desk (the whodunit's first clue). The service door's camera, if asked for, is a dummy that was never wired (m10 change 36). |
| 2 | **The staging copy of the schedule, kept on the security monitoring host.** The text beside the flag lists the channels (§4.2), the cells' early-access orders (Zero Day's among them, under Victoria's name or Vos's), and the first entries by city in plain words. | The server-hall console: the optional tasks to stop the head start and close the backdoor channel; the schedule readable (HaX reads three entries and stops); the evidence for the Victoria choice. |
| 3 | **Out of the release service's container onto the release host beneath it.** | The disarm action, armed at the server-hall console and carried to the core; the gallery stair opens only once flags 2 and 3 are in and the gallery's shutter is cut (m10 change 19, S4-11). Before it, HaX points at the bunker's own physical echo of the idea: the Cold War sealed section off the server hall, with a maintenance hatch into the machinery beneath it (R3-18). |
| 4 | **Full control of the core host**: the stolen copy, and the live line into the Citadel's vault. | His case (gated on it), and the database options at the core (§4.1). HaX's flag 4 lines carry the unsent notice on a cold start. |

Flags 1 and 2 are the earlier entry stage the brief asks for; 3 and 4 are the climax.

## 7. Teaser slot

- **Where.** m10 only, after the closing card and immediately before the final credits roll (the close runs conclusion screen, closing card, teaser, roll; m10 change 14). A few seconds. By default it runs on the endings that close on a team at 06:00 (arrest, surrender, justified lethal force, Scorched Earth) and is skipped after Join and the executed ending, where a few seconds of cosmic horror straight after the casualty curve or an execution could read as flippant (milestone M-03). The user decides when choosing the content, with all six endings in front of them. The card step and the teaser step are in the engine (F14, committed in 859da19d; §4.6): `closingCard.teaser`, off unless its `enabledBy` global is true, and m10's music entries skip it on `executed` and `joined` (m10 OT §7).
- **What.** Decided later, by the user. Nothing in m09 or m10 sets it up, refers to it, or depends on it. No other eldritch content anywhere in either mission (brief, user decision 2026-10-20).
- **How it is built.** The `closingCard.teaser` slot: lines only, no cutscene (the engine's teaser is lines; a cutscene would need engine work), behind one global, `teaser_enabled` (the slot's `enabledBy`), default `false`. The global can't vary by ending, so the skip on `executed` and `joined` is made by leaving the teaser off those endings' music entries (milestone S4–5 F-16). With it false, the credits run straight after the closing card and nothing else changes. No voiced lines until the content is decided.
- **Constraints for whoever fills it.** No new characters from this season; no line that reads as a Season 1 loose end; it must make sense after every ending it runs on.

## 9. Decision log

Sections 1–2 (premise, timeline, thread allocation) are the earlier author's. The first draft corrected one row; round 1 revised both sections where the reviews found errors or the design changed (§10).

- **§2 rows 87–89 corrected.** Row 87 put Mercer at Tomb Gamma on `mercer_fate = ko`, but an actual knock-out sets `mercer_ko` and leaves him in custody (m07 erb:228-230). Rows 87–88 now read `m07_mercer_ko` (✱) and row 89 no longer drops it.
- **Five-kind use code for imports (R/H/O/P/L).** Makes the "never gates completion" rule checkable row by row.
- **One shared ink include per mission for imports.** Fifty-odd VAR lines repeated in every ink file would drift.
- **Imported `completed` for every source.** "Never played" needs its own neutral line, and m09 may be a player's first mission.
- **No-coordinate route via `m08_completed and not m08_tomb_gamma_location_known`.** The source default is false, so the import alone can't tell a knocked-out Nightshade from an unplayed m08.
- **The deed is a task for every player.** Keeps the no-coordinate route a change of framing, not of what the player must do.
- **Static: with Voltage on `static_left and not static_left_alone`, a line on `static_left_alone`.** (Round 1, A11; replaces "either value means Static is at large".)
- **m08 export list follows inventory B §m08.2, minus `nightshade_tipped_off`, `named_on_evidence` and `suspect_theory`.** Only `nightshade_tidied` has a payoff of the tip-off pair; the other two fed the Reading's dropped entries.
- **Export additions made at stage 9, not now.** The brief forbids editing other missions in this stage; the list is ready.
- **m09 exports seven values** (`nightshade_shown_file`, `sleeper_named`, `recruiter_secret`, `recruiter_offer`, `reading_finished`, `albion_notified`, `hax_confession_answer`; §3.4). Each has a concrete m10 use; `recruiter_secret` folds m05's confession and m09's call into one value. (Superseded at stages 1–2: nine, adding `sleeper_shortlist` and `deck_pulled`; and at stage 3: eleven, adding `cage_bin` and `legacy_files_saved`, §3.4.)
- **m10 exports seven values for a later season** (§3.5). Cheap now; an ink change later.
- **All endings after flag 4.** One `concludeRequires` for every ending, and the endings are choices, not failures of the technical work.
- **Stopping the Publication is one console action, unopposed, because he wants a SAFETYNET hand on the key** (round 1, D-02). Explains the invitation, the keyed blast door, the seat at the console and the Join offer in one motive; the 06:00 fallback keeps the clock.
- **Talk-down needs two visible points, with three of the mission's own arguments always available** (D-20). Winnable by any player; carry-overs are options the player chooses.
- **His opening case uses three record lines at most, by priority** (D-08). The rest became the player's options, so the player brings up their own history.
- **"Disclose" kept as a success-path database option** (approved, F6), **with a stated cost for every option** (D-05): secure leaves flaws live, disclose burns two operations, wipe both fixes nothing.
- **Live line into the Citadel's vault, left open on purpose as a trace** (approved canon, F7; D-13).
- **Scorched Earth's chaos is his own dead-man purge** (D-12): the one outcome where he can't win the argument, wired to cover his exit.
- **Justified lethal force only when he makes a move that threatens lives, and never after a successful talk-down** (D-11, reworded at stages 1–2 to cover only the justified version). The trigger is an act, his hand going to the console where one key sends the first city's list out anyway, not a dead-man switch. The cold version follows user decision F11: a separate, clearly labelled, confirmed choice in both menus, `ending = executed` (§4.1 step 5, §4.5).
- **Every player hears the offer; Join starts only with an action, then two more confirmations** (D-21). Listening to the villain must not be a step towards betrayal; m01's LAUNCH button is the precedent.
- **Partial release comes from the m07 backdoor, not from failure or the clock.** The backdoor is present on `m07_completed and (m07_team_assignment != "trojan_horse" or m07_team_redirected)` (A1, B1).
- **Clocks never fail a mission**, in m10 (the fallback) or m09 (the purge). "Fun and educational, not hard."
- **Every presence import has a default occupant, and every at-large value that equals a default also needs `completed`** (A5).
- **Five full returning scenes in m10 (Nightshade, Victoria, Voltage, Ghost, Gary); the rest are presences, props or lines** (D-01). The five carry the most player choices each; Mercer, Reeves and Hollis become presence and props.
- **Lengths: m09 about 75 minutes, m10 about 100** (D-01, orchestrator). The old outline's ranges; m07 ships at 90. (Superseded at stages 1–2, orchestrator rulings: m09 about 85 with the field guides, 95–100 without; m10 about 105, ceiling 110.)
- **The sleeper is a new Task Force Null agent, introduced in m09 and found by a whodunit** (D-03). Making Vector or Phantom the sleeper would undo m08's verdict; meeting her in m09 gives the betrayal something to betray.
- **She came in as a lateral hire through Harwick & Lowe, an older front, not in Tesseract's intake** (D-03, A24, B3; the round 1 TalentStack route is withdrawn). Explains why the post-m08 screening missed her and why m05's lists never held her.
- **Renamed to Halina Kowalczyk, a woman** (A2, D-19). Wierzbicki is already an m05 name; the change also balances the new cast.
- **A wrong accusation costs an innocent's place on the team, not a life.** Accusing must be a real choice with a cost; the m10 evidence always allows a right answer.
- **The sleeper wounds Quintero rather than killing anyone.** A missed optional task should cost, but a death would read as punishment.
- **Victoria's loyalty is decided by an m10 choice (trust, confront, refuse), not by `sterling_on_call_seen`** (A4). That latch records exploring, not conduct; it now drives only the escaped branch's threat, which is what it means.
- **Every m09 player gets the Recruiter's offer** (D-09). A real mid-mission choice for a player on default branches. (Stage 3: priced at the whole deck.)
- **m09's own clock is the purge, costing optional finds only** (D-10, D-24). Pressure inside the mission, a job for Sorensen and Tran, and the trigger for cutting the leave short.
- **The Albion flaw catalogued and never reported is found in m09** (D-04). The player meets the truth in Tesseract's case before he makes it, and the m04 choice lands twice.
- **The Reading capped at four entries plus the prediction, and Sorensen quotes one aloud** (D-06).
- **Tier the imports, keep the list broad** (D-07, orchestrator). Tier 3 cuts only what lost its payoff (the Reading's dropped entries, `casualties_occurred`); both approved ink changes stay in Tier 1 or 2.
- **One power-room phone slot (a captured Voltage kept on the phone, else Vance, else Elena, else HaX), and one non-HaX voice per room; Kemal a line; Gary's m09 call gives a hint the player uses** (D-17, R2-01, R3-01). (Superseded, round 4 N-02 and milestone MS-07: a captured Voltage is in the room, so the phone slot is Vance, else Elena, else HaX.)
- **The wall of cards is the "previously on", with never-played cards** (D-15, A17).
- **m09's briefing is a cutscene; m10 has nine playable rooms** (D-16).
- **Tesseract's origin stays unplaced** (A15). The voice bible keeps it deliberately vague; nothing in the finale needs a birthplace.
- **Tesseract calls HaX, not the player, at the end of m09.** He is her teacher; the player overhearing one side is more tense than a monologue.
- **HaX's meaning of the line is told in m09.** It makes the line the player's own argument in m10 (row 120). (Changed at stages 1–2: in m09 it is cut to three words and one sentence after the deck; in m10 it is a record answer on `m09_completed`, and the third exchange's mission rebuttal is the clean-hands argument, m10 change 31.)
- **Netherton says the name in m09 (row 125)**, in words that work whether or not the player heard his optional m08 pledge (A27, B5). The scene where the proof comes together is his.
- **New cast varied in age, background and sex**, clear of the Chen/Reeves/Park/Okafor/Wierzbicki clashes, every name grepped (A2, D-19).
- **Mercer's absence filled by Asante, not an empty hall.** The server hall needs someone who can explain the release.
- **The two dead agents named (Fraser, Ferreira).** Row 100 has Netherton read the names, so they need names.
- **Teaser behind a default-off global.** Planned slot, no dependency (brief).
- **Clocks in UTC only.** Avoids the local-midnight/dawn mismatch in §1.5.
- **Titles: m09 *Digital Archaeology* (kept); m10 *Full Disclosure*** (user's choice, 2026-10-20; "Tomb Gamma" declined, F8), id `m10_full_disclosure`.

**Round 2** (orchestrator decisions applied as given; the rest are mine):

- **Every won branch puts a person in the bunker; five slots filled on every combination** (R2-01, orchestrator). Captured Voltage on the plant phone, protected Gary as consultant, arrested Nightshade in cuffs with a deal, arrested Victoria bargaining from custody, Ghost for everyone. A careful player's m10 is as full as a careless one's. (Superseded, rounds 3–4: each won branch has a decision and a face-to-face beat in its own room, §5.3; only the door slot is at the forward post.)
- **Pieter Vos fills Victoria's desk** when she is KO'd or never met. Pays m03's "filled by Monday" and keeps the gallery a scene on every branch.
- **He stops the clock when 0x00 walks in; flag 3 moves to the server hall** (R2-02, orchestrator). The climax is talk and choices; the clock presses on the raid only. (Superseded, round 3: the countdown runs on story events, §4.2. Superseded again at stages 1–2: he pauses nothing, "It goes at six whatever we say.", m10 change 37.)
- **He refuses WREN at the key** (R2-03). Turns the hole in his motive into its clearest statement.
- **Three clues, an alibi and the player's own cracker serial; a wrong accusation is visible and undoable; a knock-out is not an accusation** (R2-04, R2-06, orchestrator). (Clue 3 superseded at stages 1–2: the bench's cloner record and the breaching case's sign-out sheet; the cracker is a suspicion beat and the accusation's last card, m10 change 22.)
- **WREN came in through Harwick & Lowe, an older front** (B3, orchestrator). SAFETYNET has known TalentStack since m05; an older firm explains the miss.
- **The head start goes out at 04:00, on the same clock** (R2-08). The cells' fate becomes a payoff of m02 and m03 choices. (Superseded, rounds 3–4: found at flag 2, goes as the gallery door opens unless stopped at the server hall. Superseded again at stages 1–2: it goes as the outer lock opens, m10 change 19, §4.2.)
- **Tier 1 is every import that changes a room; Tier 2 is lines only** (R2-09, orchestrator). m09 19 + 24, m10 37 + 25 (26 after round 3 added `m05_final_choice` for C2).
- **The purge runs on story events, with two save-one choices** (R2-10, orchestrator). VM speed costs nothing; the loss is one the player picked.
- **m10 gets a cold-start briefing; never-played canon is stated once and matches the defaults** (R2-11, R2-16, B1, B2).
- **Disclose burns operations the player knows; the player sends Tesseract's unsent vendor notice in m09** (R2-12, orchestrator). Secure gains the operations; disclose costs them; coordinated disclosure is done once in play before the menu.
- **The power room is on the main path** (R2-13): the gallery's shutter runs off it. (Called "the plant room" before round 3.)
- **The talk-down is three exchanges, only the matching answer lands, two of three to win** (R2-15). Weak answers are visibly weak.
- **HaX's confession gets an answer that decides whether she speaks at the core** (R2-19).
- **The Recruiter's file holds the handler list; her name is always right; the double-cross is allowed** (R2-20).
- **Ghost rings the server hall and is selling the head start; the parley's fact opens a window past the outer lock** (R2-21, weak Tier 1 #3). (Superseded, round 3: the handset is in the security office.)
- **The Albion flaw is one of m04's holes, catalogued nine years ago** (B4, R2-07, orchestrator).
- **Hollis's prop stays on `evaded` only** (B9). One value, no new export, for a prop.
- **VM wording follows the orchestrator's story-level text** (two machines in m09; m10 flags 1–3 reworded). The VM plan itself stays with the Haiku agent.

**Round 3** (orchestrator decisions applied as given; the rest are mine):

- **Each won branch gets one decision and one face-to-face beat; one non-HaX voice per room** (R3-01, orchestrator). Captured Voltage: walk him to his switchgear or keep him on the phone. Protected Gary: in person at the workshop, talk round or take in the technician he trained. Refused Ghost: a question back, then the window or a camera blind. Arrested Victoria: face to face at the forward post. (Staging superseded, round 4, N-02: only the door slot is at the forward post; Voltage is placed at the power room; Victoria rings the server hall and the face-to-face beat is Vos in the gallery.) Arrested Nightshade's deal knows the player said "no deals".
- **The raid countdown runs on story events** (R3-02, orchestrator). The head start goes only if the gallery door opens before the player stops it at the server hall (round 4, N-05; the outer lock since stages 1–2); Seattle's batch only through an open backdoor; 06:00 never fires before the core.
- **The purge choice is forced by an event** (R3-04, orchestrator): the cage opening takes the other bin; the end of the Recruiter's call starts the wipe. Stopping the person saves only what is left. Her offer and the tape-deck choice are one decision.
- **One purge order in all three documents: flag 2 before her call** (C3, orchestrator). The ledger reveals WREN before the Recruiter can offer her name.
- **02:10 is a pre-breach recon split; the readers accept SAFETYNET badges because he loaded them from the stolen database; WREN propped the service door for her own exit** (R3-03, C4, orchestrator). Quintero and Kowalczyk took the tunnel; Arakawa was at the fence with the player. (Changed at stages 1–2: the door is Tesseract's exit and her way back to the key, not an exit with the stolen copy; WREN has one goal, the key, m09 change 8, m10 change 27.)
- **m07's never-played canon is "one team, four sites; Austin reached; two of ours died at the others"** (C1, orchestrator). Matches m08's shipped line and row 100. Gameplay defaults kept; the dark media room now has its own reason.
- **The Recruiter's "network" is gated by m05** (C2, orchestrator): placements by default, handlers and the forty-seven on exposure, Torres' missing names on the turned branch. m10 imports `m05_final_choice` for the epilogue.
- **Disclose's defaults are operations still live after the raid (the watch on Ghost's buyers, the Zero Day broker tap; round 4 replaced the handler watch), and every database option has an epilogue scene beat** (R3-05, orchestrator).
- **The double-cross rolls up only part of her network** (R3-07). Three answers, three outcomes.
- **Ghost's handset moves to the security office** (R3-12). One scene per room.
- **The notice is nine years late** (C11, R3-11). It was drafted with the memo.
- **"Power room", never "plant", for the room in player-facing text** (R3-10).
- **The safe-opened branch gets its own HaX confession; `should_have_told` gets its own beat at the core** (C18, R3-16). Neither answer is punished. (Stages 1–2: the forms are labelled "m06 name found / not found", and a third answer, `afterwards`, is added; m09 changes 19, 24.)
- **L+ is defined as a scene-shaping line built with Tier 1** (C16). Tier 1 counts unchanged.

**Stages 1–2** (arc-keeper, 2026-10-21; orchestrator rulings applied as given; each change is listed in §15):

- **The stage 1–2 documents are the authority where they and this one disagreed.** They are newer and reviewed four rounds each.
- **The Publication has two modes, and the casualty sheet prices the key** (m09 change 28, m10 change 42). City by city from 06:00; everywhere in the same minute by key. 1,100–2,600 is the key's price; the fallback kills fewer, later. It answers "why build a key at all?" and keeps the 06:00 clock honest.
- **WREN has one goal, the key** (orchestrator ruling). The Architect took her tasking for tonight; "plays both ends" and the copy for the Initiative are withdrawn everywhere.
- **The cracker canon is m10's** (orchestrator ruling, m10 R3-06): Kowalczyk's own, bench-made, logged by batch; the cloner is the unit logged "to WREN"; m09 claims nothing about where it was made.
- **No pause at the core** (m10 change 37). "Paused" contradicted the 06:00 fallback.
- **The head start goes at the outer lock** (m10 change 19, S1-01). Every gallery decision about Zero Day's share comes before it.
- **Two lethal endings** (F11). D-11 reworded to the justified version only.
- **One forced-phone-scene rule for both missions** (orchestrator ruling; §6.1).
- **Per-scene one-voice rule in m09, per-room in m10** (m09 change 6). m09's scenes are phone-heavy in shared rooms; m10's raid moves room by room.
- **The cross-reference narrows WREN to two; only the Recruiter names her** (m09 change 6). Keeps her offer a real trade and gives m10 a helper (`sleeper_shortlist`).
- **The m09 briefing holds the name back** (m09 change 1). The identity scene gets its weight.
- **`m09_deck_pulled` is Tier 2 in m10** (arc-keeper's call): its only m10 use is a record answer. (Superseded at stage 3: Tier 1, a helper, orchestrator ruling; §16.)

**Stage 3** (arc-keeper, 2026-10-21; user decisions F12 and F13 and the orchestrator's rulings applied as given; each change is listed in §16):

- **The stage 3 documents are the authority where they and this one disagreed**, with m10 MC winning for m10-side behaviour and m09 MC for m09-side.
- **The Recruiter's price is the whole deck** (m09 MC ch. 1). Take: nothing comes off. Refuse: HaX saves one tape. No "pull nothing". Every cost voiced on the call.
- **The double-cross voids the name** (m09 MC C-02, R2-01, R2-02; orchestrator ruling). She warns WREN; Kowalczyk walks off the apron at Helena; in m10 WREN is off the team and waits at the core, where nobody is wounded. That clean core is the double-cross's one gain.
- **`recruiter_secret = denied`** (m09 MC C-12). Three answers on the sold-and-lied branch; m05 canon is that no money changed hands.
- **Eleven m09 exports; m10 imports 67 (Tier 1 40, Tier 2 27)** (orchestrator rulings; m09 MC §10, m10 MC P8). `m09_deck_pulled` and `m09_recruiter_offer` move up to Tier 1; `m09_cage_bin`, `m09_legacy_files_saved` are Tier 2. m09 imports 44 with `m04_crew_lost`.
- **The Alpha tapes are voiced** (F12) and pay three times in m10: a record answer, the blast-door line, and the service-door warning that spares Quintero.
- **The notice is every player's decision, and costs the Critical Mass watch** (m09 MC ch. 6–7, R2-04, R2-05). The flaw is in the vendor's controller; disclosure runs at its true speed in both missions.
- **One list of six operations for the database choice, and one cells ledger** (m10 MC P3, P33, P52). Two operations always apply; surrender always gets two cells.
- **The console kills one share, or traces one** (m10 MC P29, R2-01; orchestrator ruling). Every live share goes at the outer lock's unlock event.
- **F13: surrender is a deal with no public trial; arrest a public trial with no cells** (user decision, 2026-10-21). Netherton says so before the menu.
- **The cold choice is always last, and "Lower the weapon." removes it** (m10 MC P18; orchestrator ruling).
- **The $12.8M goes to the victims' fund, except on `secured`, where the Council keeps it as evidence** (orchestrator ruling; P9), on every ending that reaches 06:00 except the cold one.
- **The Recruiter has no card line of her own** (m10 MC §6.2): her state reaches the close through the ledger and "Elsewhere". m10's version is used over m09 MC's "closing card" wording (§16, conflicts).
- **Tesseract can be knocked out only as a fallback, and the core still ends on a pick that sets `ending`** (m10 MC P48, R3-01).

**Stage 6** (arc-keeper, 2026-10-21; the orchestrator's rulings applied as given; each change is listed in §20):

- **The in-world year is 2026**, and no string prints the present month (orchestrator ruling; §1.5).
- **One set of shared facts for both missions**: the bunker is the Sun River Regional Control Site, leased to Big Sky Holdings LLC from June 2, 2016; the Albion flaw is Carrow Controls' RM-4 module, VH-2017-0312, "Disposition: RETAIN. Operational value."; the casualty sheet is word for word in both, with "UTC" on the fallback line (§1.2, §1.3).
- **The cracker canon is m10's**, in Citadel Incident File V-47, the bench log and the cracker's description; m09 carries none (row 29).
- **Names**: Lyle Dillard (m09's called guard), Sione Fonoti (m10's lock guard), D. Varga (Tran's handler), LINNET (Noor's codename) (§5.2).
- **No `||` in any engine condition**; the close's "or"s and counts are computed once in ink by `close_compute` (coordinator ruling; §4.6).
- **The close**: five card lines in different shapes, a person last on every save; ELSEWHERE in three movements ending on a person; the telescope line closes ELSEWHERE, or the card when it has no other human line; Join ends on "Zero Day: still trading." (§4.6).
- **Elena Torres can reach the card**: the forty-seven take the human line only while some were not reached in time (orchestrator ruling on m10 S6-11; §4.6, row 62).
- **A password lock on Asante's model** (`model_terminal`, `DOUBLECOUNT`), so the optional decode can register (orchestrator ruling on m10 S6-02; §4.1).
- **Tesseract recruited Nightshade at 23** is canon, user-confirmed, in the bible (40f0f219; §8 r17).
